--[p136]-- Collet stroke This sets the distance in mils (.001 inch) that the collet moves down from the offset point during the pick stroke.

===== [p413-413] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Carrier Tape Supply Reel Tension Adjustment (2块) =====
--[p413]-- To make a tension adjustment, loosen the locking screw on the tension adjustment. Turn the adjusting knob manually. (To increase the tension, use a setting with a higher number.)
2. Once the tension setting is correct, tighten the locking screw.
--[p413]-- Carrier Tape Supply Reel Tension Adjustment
The tension of the supply reel should be set to the minimum force that will hold the carrier tape tightly against the indexer. The carrier tape between the indexer and the supply reel should not sag. Tapes which are wide or thick are heavier, and will need more tension than thin ones. The tensioning device is located on the rear side of the hub which supports the supply reel. See Fig. 5A-5.

5. The setting for Color affects the way the image from the camera is presented and interpreted. Choosing the B setting presents an image with black edge features on a white background. Choosing W presents an image with white edge features on a black background.

--[p338]-- Training for Edge Inspection
1. Once the edge damage inspection feature has been turned on, the next job is to set up the parameters used by the inspection system for all of the ROI's included in this inspection. The fields for this are located in the upper left-hand corner of the screen.
2. The Brightness and Contrast values affect the image presented by the camera. By adjusting these two controls, you can help to highlight just the features which are most important to the inspection.
--[p345]-- The Pass entry shows whether the system thinks this setup will be usable.
The message at the bottom of the screen may indicate that the system did not accept the training setup for this ROI. In this case the system may suggest a different threshold to use in setting up the gray-scale for this image.
--[p342]-- During input inspection, the camera creates a black-and-white image of the die, and the system analyzes it using an inspection algorithm. For the purposes of the inspection, the algorithm has to see every area of the image as either “white” or “black.” All of the gray areas are identified as one of these two extremes. The setting used for Threshold tells the system where to make the distinction between “white” and “black.”
Start with a value of 32.
--[p340]-- To set the size of the ROI, click on the Size button, then use the X and Y buttons to change the dimensions. You can also enter X and Y dimensions in the boxes, then use the Enter key to enter the new values.
To change the position of the ROI, click on the Position button, and use the X and Y buttons to make adjustments. Position the ROI exactly around the area to be inspected. (If the inspection area is larger than necessary, this will slow down the machine.)
7.
--[p339]-- In this case we want to highlight the edges of the die. Start by setting the Brightness control to 160, and the Contrast control to 10.
3. For this type of inspection, select the Input camera.
4. The Edge Parameters settings tell the system how to interpret the pictures of the die edges. The entry for Area Variation determines how closely the feature used for an inspection has to match the original feature on the die you used for training.
--[p343]-- In the lower center of the screen, click on the Train button. This tells the system to use the information you have provided to set up the model for the ROI currently being displayed.
10. To check the ROI, click on the Test button. The system will return a score which gives an indication of how reliable the model will be.
11. To create an additional ROI for this inspection type, press the Insert ROI button. The system will ask whether you really want to use an extra ROI.
--[p343]-- Click on Yes to continue adding another ROI.

The entry for Detect % for each ROI shows the percentage of the ROI area which the vision system recognized as damaged. The entry for Maximum Deviation shows the difference in
size between the largest and smallest percentages which appeared in the ROI.
--[p340]-- So far, we have been dealing with parameters that apply to all of the ROI's used in this inspection. The next job is to set up the individual ROI(s). For Edge Damage Detection, you usually use four narrow ROI's - one for each edge of the die. See Fig. 5-29. Notice the arrow buttons, and the Size and Position buttons in the lower center of the screen. These are used to set the size and position of the ROI. The outline of the ROI will appear on the image presented by the input camera.
--[p341]-- The setting for Threshold for an ROI is important because it tells the system how to tell the difference between the black and white areas in the search image. The system uses a grayscale with 64 levels. An area which is pure white is represented by a gray-scale value of 63, and pure black is represented by 0. A
value of 32 falls in the middle and represents a medium gray.
--[p342]-- Later on, the Training software may suggest a different value. Generally if the system suggests a number which is near one of the extremes (0 or 63), the camera is not getting a good image and the lighting should be adjusted.
8. Set the entry for Detect % for the ROI. This entry is the allowable percentage of the area identified as "damaged" within that ROI. If the vision system sees a larger area of damage in an ROI, it will reject that die.

--[p411]-- For this type of tape, the tape is held in the correct position side-to-side by a series of guide pins. These can be mounted in two rows of holes in the guide plates. There are a total of ten pins - five on each side. Install the ten guide pins.
6. Slide the carrier tape through the sprocket hole sensor. (The sprocket holes should be toward the front.) Continue to slide the tape through the cover tape rollers and under the heat seal shoes.
7.
--[p412]-- On the tape index mechanism, lift the drive roller and support it on the cam.
8. Hand wrap the carrier tape onto the take-up reel, then switch on the take-up motor. Lift the drive roller motor off of the support cam and lower the drive roller.
9. Position the carrier tape so that the sides of the carrier tape fit between the guide pins.

3. Feed the carrier tape under the paddle for the tension control device.
4. Feed the carrier tape through the tape guides on the lower right-hand side of the indexer.

===== [p56-59] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 4.4 TURNING ON THE MACHINE (6块) =====
--[p56]-- 4.4 TURNING ON THE MACHINE
In this section, we will assume that the machine has already been set up. The doors should be shut, and the machine should be ready to run. (For detailed setup instructions, see Section 5 of this manual.)
1. Before starting the machine, be sure that there are no “extra” items inside the machine which could prevent the parts of the machine from moving freely. Check for any tools or other objects which seem to be out of place.
2.
--[p59]-- Before the machine will operate, it must return all of the moving parts to their starting or Home positions. Again, check to be sure that nothing will stop the parts of the machine from moving. If the machine is free to move, click on the OK button using the left button on the mouse. (You can also press the Enter key on the keyboard.) Once you start the home sequence, all of the parts of the machine will move to their home positions.
8.
--[p58]-- The control computer will start, and will automatically load the software used to run the machine. (This will take about 1.5 minutes. If the password feature is enabled, the computer may ask you to enter a password at this point.) During the loading process, the indicator on top of the machine will flash. When the machine has finished loading the software, the following message will appear in the center of the screen:
7.
--[p59]-- If one of the doors is not closed, an error message will appear. See Fig. 4-3. Close the door and press "OK" to continue.
--[p57]-- The air regulator is mounted on the front of the unit. Check that the factory air supply is turned on. The regulator should be set to 85 to 100 p.s.i. If necessary, change the setting using the black knob next to the gauge.
3. If the unit has an input wafer elevator - Check to be sure that the cassette has been loaded correctly.
--[p57]-- If the optional bar code reader is in use, the bar code label for each frame should be visible on the left side of the cassette, as the front of the cassette is inserted into the machine. Be sure that each wafer frame is pushed back into the cassette. Mount the cassette firmly in position on the elevator. Swing the frame sensor into position behind the cassette.
4. Turn on the main power switch. See Fig. 1-9.
5. On the control panel, press the green Start button. See Fig. 1-5.
6 .

--[p366]-- Checking the Inspection
1. The next step in the process is to turn off the die inspection function, and work on the picking function. To turn off die inspection, go back to the Mark Detection screen (Fig. 5-37).
2. Once the picking function is working correctly, turn on die inspection again. Return to the Mark Detection screen (Fig. 5-37). Now you can check the way the machine actually performs the inspection. Go back to the Run mode and start the job.
--[p324]-- Checking the Inspection
1. The next step in the process is to turn off the die inspection function, and work on the picking function. To turn off die inspection, go back to the Bump Inspection screen (Fig. 5-31).
2. Once the picking function is working correctly, turn on die inspection again. Return to the Bump Inspection screen. Now you can check the way the machine actually performs the inspection. Go back to the Run mode and start the job. From the Tune Options screen, select Single Step.
This will allow you to step through the whole pick-and-place cycle, including the die inspection process.
--[p345]-- Checking the Inspection
1. The next step in the process is to turn off the die inspection function, and work on the picking function. To turn off die inspection, go back to the Edge Damage Detection screen (Fig. 5- 34).
2. Once the picking function is working correctly, turn on die inspection again. Return to the
Edge Damage Detection screen (Fig. 5-34). Now you can check the way the machine actually performs the inspection. Go back to the Run mode and start the job.

===== [p122-122] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Danger! (2块) =====
--[p122]-- Danger!
On a unit which is equipped with an "internal" U.P.S., after the Stop button or an Emergency Stop switch is pressed, the U.P.S. will continue to supply power to the machine. After two minutes, the U.P.S. will send a signal to power-off the PC and PC monitor. After an additional three minutes, the U.P.S. itself will power off. (To resume operation, press the green Power On button.) An operator should be aware that the U.P.S.
--[p122]-- may still be supplying power to the machine, even though the Stop or Emergency Stop button has been pressed.
2. To restart after an E-stop, release and pull out the E-stop switch and press the green Start
button. The computer on the machine will boot, then home all of the mechanical parts. Reopen the job and restart.

===== [p210-210] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Multi Row Offset (1块) =====
--[p210]-- Multi Row Offset
This is used in conjunction with Multi-Row Pitch. This entry vertically shifts the row groups in the generic map from the center (home) of the wafer by the amount programmed. The setting can range from -3000 to 3000 mils. A positive number offsets toward the top of the map.

===== [p40-42] Transfer arm - (3块) =====
--[p40]-- Transfer arm -
At the beginning of the transfer process, one side of the transfer arm picks up each die. The die is picked and held by a vacuum operating through a "collet." The “collet” is a small hollow tube at the tip of the pick mechanism. This is the part which actually touches the top of the die. The size of the collet must be matched with the size and weight of the die. A larger, heavier die will require a collet with a
Anvil Mechanism
After a die is picked, the arm rotates 180°.
--[p42 [表]]-- These are chosen to match the size and weight of the die being picked. (A larger die usually requires more needles to provide the correct lifting action.)
Pressure switches - These switches allow the machine to sense the presence of a die on the two collets on the inverter
(collet 3 and collet 4). Reject vacuum unit - | This device can be set up to collect any die which fail output inspection, rather than placing them. 45
--[p41]-- The vacuum holds the die firmly on the collet. Finally, the collet is lowered into the place position, and the vacuum is shut off. A puff of air releases the die onto the output device.
Anvil and needle assemblies -
During picking, the anvil is designed to move up and support the bottom of the wafer. At the center of the anvil, one or more needles rise up to lift the die away from the wafer film. The anvil assembly may be fitted with one or more needles.

===== [p481-481] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Table 5A-8 - Inverter Parameters (1块) =====
--[p481]-- Table 5A-8 - Inverter Parameters
Parameter Description
Inverter Stroke This sets the distance that each inverter collet moves as it deposits the die.

===== [p123-124] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 4.12 EMERGENCY STOP - EXTERNAL U.P.S. (4块) =====
--[p124]-- Because of the way the system was stopped, it also lost the map information that records which die have been picked, and which remain. To complete the process, you must tell the
computer where on the wafer to start picking. Go to the LCAD screen (Fig. 4-14) and use the "Start At" function to identify the next die to be picked (Fig. 4-15).
--[p123]-- (The red Stop switch on the operator’s panel acts as an Emergency Stop.) Once you press any of the E-Stop switches, an error message will appear, and the red indicator on top of the machine will light.
1. The E-Stop switch will cut off power to the motors, and will stop all pneumatic functions. The computer which supervises the machine will continue to run. The computer will shut down in a random way. This means that some of the supervisory files may be lost.
--[p123]-- When the machine is restarted, may skip over some die.
2. To restart after an E-stop, pull out the E-stop switch and press the green Start button. The computer on the machine will boot, then home all of the mechanical parts.
3. Because the computer "lost" the supervisory files for the job, you must take some additional steps to restart. Because of the way the system was stopped, it lost the alignment information for the wafer. This means you must do a manual alignment of the wafer.
4.
--[p123]-- 4.12 EMERGENCY STOP - EXTERNAL U.P.S.
Some units have an "uninterruptible power supply" which is installed in the power line running to the machine (an "external" U.P.S.) On this type of machine, you can use any of the Emergency Stop switches to stop the machine quickly in an emergency. There are three of these switches.

===== [p81-84] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Input Map File (5块) =====
--[p81]-- Input Map File
This is a map of a specific wafer, including information on which die have been picked, how many remain, and so on.
Job This field can also be used by the MIS software. Each job can include one or more wafers. (See Section 6 for more information.)
Output The output file tells the machine how to handle the output side of the process during placing.
--[p84]-- Total This indicates how many die the operator plans to place on the tape or other output device.
Current Sort A single wafer can include die of different types or "bin" or "sort" values. This field shows the sort value of the die currently being picked.
--[p83]-- Barcode This field indicates the bar code of the wafer currently being processed.
Lot ID As we said, a "shop order" can include one or more jobs or lots. If you are using the MIS system to track performance information, enter the Lot ID here. (See Section 6 for more information.)
Die Picked This is the total number of die picked from the current wafer.
Skipped This indicates the total number of die were skipped (not picked) because of mispicks or ink dots.
--[p82]-- Output Map The output map includes specific information about the die which have been placed on one output device - one reel of tape, or one waffle pack, for example.
Use Barcode On a machine with the optional , each wafer or frame on the input side of the machine can be identified by a bar code. Enable the option using this entry.
Current Slot On a unit with the automatic , this indicates the slot in the input elevator which supplied the wafer currently being processed.
--[p84]-- Ink Dots This field shows how many die on the current wafer were not picked because they had already been marked with ink dots.
Missed Each time the machine attempts to pick a die and does not succeed, this number increases by one.
Die Present This indicates the number of die remaining on the current wafer.
Die Placed This field shows how many die from the current wafer have been successfully placed.

===== [p101-499] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Note (59块) =====
--[p423]-- On each side, the speed of the shoes contacting the cover tape must be adjusted so that both of the shoes hit the tape at the same time. If the down speed on either side is too fast, this can jar any uncovered die out of their pockets. The up and down speeds can be adjusted using flow controls. The flow control for the front cylinder is located on the front cylinder itself. The flow control for the rear cylinder is located at the rear of the indexer, below the terminal block.
5.
--[p287]-- Note
The instructions here apply to ink dot inspection when it is used on the input side of the machine. Ink dot inspection can also be used on the output side of the machine, but the function is different. When used on the output side, ink dot inspection is used as a backup for street alignment. For details, see Section 5A, 5B or 5C.
When using ink dot inspection on the input side of the machine -
There are three ways the machine can be set up to run in the Ink Dot mode:
--[p116]-- Note
The individual film frames in the cassette must be in the correct orientation. This ensures that pin 1 on each die is in a predictable position. Each film frame has two notched corners - these face the rear of the machine when the frame has been loaded. When placing the film frames in the cassette, be sure these notched corners are in the correct locations. See Fig. 5C-3 for more information.
--[p202]-- Note
If the value for Wafer Diameter is incorrect, the system will not display the correct number of die on a wafer.
12. When you are finished making changes to these parameters, click on the OK button. The system will return to the LCad screen shown in Fig. 5-3.
--[p291]-- When you have set up the last model, click on the OK button. In the pull-down menu in the upper left-hand corner of the screen, select the Street Model.
12. Click on the button labeled Auto Scale. The Auto Scale function relates the image from the vision system (measured in pixels) to the controls for the mechanical sections of the machine (measured in thousandths of an inch). The test uses the Street model.
--[p426]-- Note
The "Take-Up On/Off" switch has a set of contacts which indicate the switch position to the computer. The indexer and system will not operate with the switch in the off position.
--[p291]-- The system aligns the camera with the Street model, then moves through a pattern, along both the X and Y axes, which establishes the relationship between the two measurement systems. Normally the system returns a Pass indication if the Street model is OK. If the system does not pass the Auto Scale procedure, you will have to re-teach the Street model.
13. Next, click on Auto Pitch.
--[p288]-- In this case, you train with a die which does have an ink dot.
Ink Dot Blob - This method is useful when the dot areas are distorted, and unpredictable. This type of search looks at the ratio between two kinds of pixels in the search area. The pixels covered by the ink dot are identified as "black", and the uncovered pixels on the die are identified as "white." A threshold setting tells the vision system how to tell the difference.
--[p287]-- Train Good Die - Train the system to recognize an area of a die which does not have an ink dot on it. When the machine is in the run mode, and a die with an ink dot appears, the dot will block out the part of the image which was used as the Ink Dot model. When this happens, the computer returns a Minimum Quality score which is too low, and the machine rejects the die.
Train Ink Dot - Use this option when there is nothing on the die to use as a model.
--[p292]-- At the beginning of the teaching process, you will recall that you gave the system some information about the die pitch. You also tested the entries for the X pitch and Y pitch to be sure they were approximately correct. The Auto Pitch feature allows the system to fine-tune the die pitch measurements. This test also uses the Street model.
During the test, the unit steps back and forth over a series of die in both the X and Y axes. Again, the system should return a Pass indication.
--[p283]-- Note
Remember to jog out any Theta that may be in the wafer before teaching the Theta 1 and Theta 2 models. Failure to do so may result in poor wafer alignment and poor placement when the machine runs the program.
Teaching Theta 1 and Theta 2 Models

This is called a “one acquire,” and it can speed up the processing rate.

===== [p30-35] SECS connection - (5块) =====
--[p35]-- When the guard is raised, the arm operates at a pre-set slow speed. This safety feature is useful during maintenance.
The wafers are mounted in a carrier called a “cassette.” Each cassette can hold up to 25 wafers.
As the unit selects different wafers, the elevator moves the cassette up or down. This places the desired wafer at the correct height where it can be reached by the loader arm. The left edge of each wafer frame can carry a bar code. This can identify the die on that particular wafer.
The machine uses the bar code information to call up the correct wafer map from the network or hard disk drive.
--[p33 [表]]-- The regulator sets the air pressure used by the machine, and the gauge indicates the pressure. Normally, this adjustment should be set to 85 psi.
largement or “zoom&quot; can be adjusted using the up- per adjusting ring on each lens. Light source - The area below each camera is lighted by an illumi- nation source and a fiber-optic cable. Transfer arm safety guard -
When the guard is in the lowered (working) posi- tion, the transfer arm operates at the normal speed.
--[p31]-- Reset button - If the computer ever encounters a problem, one way to recover is to press the Reset button. This
will bring the computer back to the beginning of the start-up process. (Note - Resetting the computer will cause it to lose any current operating informa- tion.)
Power switch - This controls power to the computer only. It should normally be left on at all times.
CD-ROM drive -
This unit may be used to store backup copies of process information.
Fig. 1-9
Main Power Switch
--[p32]-- Main power switch -
This controls the power to the machine. (Even if this switch is turned on, the machine will not operate. The Start/Reset button must be pressed first.)
Ground connection -
This connection provides a good ground connection. During service procedures involving components which are sensitive to static electricity, the technician can attach an anti-static wrist strap here.
System air regulator and gauge -
--[p30 [表]]-- SECS connection -
This optional connection connects to a supervisory network. The machine can use this connection to download detailed information on particular wafers (.MAP files) which are stored in the host computer.
Computer - The unit is supervised by an on-board computer. The computer includes a “hard disk” memory which carries all of the Laurier control software which op-
erates the machine.

===== [p394-394] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 3. If the output tape reaches the programmed number of die - (2块) =====
--[p394]-- 3. If the output tape reaches the programmed number of die -
As it operates, the DS-9000 keeps track of the number of die placed on the output reel. When the software determines that the correct number of die have been placed, a status message will appear on the screen: "Pocketed tape is full."
To stop the job, the operator clicks on "N to quit." The computer will ask, "Do you want to make a trailer?" A "trailer" is a section of cover tape which extends beyond the tail end of the carrier tape.
--[p394]-- Answer "Y" or "N" as required.
To complete the process, the operator presses the Tape Index button to bring the end of the carrier tape out of the machine, and then cuts the tape.
For instructions on re-threading the carrier tape, see section 5A.3.

===== [p402-405] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5A.5 THEORY OF OPERATION (9块) =====
--[p404]-- Once the die have been placed in the pockets, the cover tape is applied to the carrier tape. The supply roll for the cover tape is carried on a platter with a centering hub. The platter, with the cover tape supply roll, is secured to a magnetic tensioning clutch using a hand nut. The cover tape then passes around a peel-off arm, past the cover tape out sensor, around a vertical roller and over a stepped horizontal roller.
--[p402]-- 5A.5 THEORY OF OPERATION
Carrier tape is supplied to the indexer from a reel located at the bottom of the feed/ take-up unit. It is fed into the mechanism upside-down, and reversed just before the die are placed. After component placement the tape can be inspected by the vision system. If necessary, cover tape is applied and heat-sealed. As the tape is advanced, the take-up reel rewinds the tape onto a reel located at the top of the reel feed/take-up unit.
--[p402]-- The additional length of tape reduces the tension, allowing the paddle to lower, and de-energizing the air cylinder.
The tape is fed upside down into the indexer through two tape guides. These hold the tape tight against the indexer. The guides also keep a consistent tension between the indexer and the supply reel. The tape reverses around the nose of the indexer and enters the top of the indexer.
--[p403]-- At this point, the pocket openings are oriented up and the sprocket holes are facing towards the front of the system.
Directly across from the paddle is the sprocket hole sensor assembly. This assembly consists of a thrubeam sensor pair mounted on an X-Y stage. The sensor is used to “count” sprocket holes and set the index pitch. The sensor can be adjusted in the cross tape direction, (along the Y axis) to accommodate various pocket widths.
--[p404]-- It can also be adjusted along the length of the tape (along the X axis) to precisely adjust the position of the tape after an index.
After a die has been placed, the tape will be indexed to the next pocket. After the tape has been indexed enough times so that the die has advanced 32 mm, the die reaches the inspection zone. The pocket can be examined by the vision system for positional deviation and surface defects.
--[p404]-- Next, the cover tape feeds into a guide mechanism which steers the cover tape onto the carrier tape. Both the carrier tape and the cover tape pass together between a set of pinch rollers. If the unit is running pressure-sensitive cover tape, the pinch rollers seal the tapes together. If the unit is processing heat-activated cover tape, a pair of heat-seal shoes seal the tape. These shoes are positioned immediately following the pinch rollers, and are pneumatically-actuated.
--[p402]-- The tape is tensioned on the supply reel by an adjustable magnetic tensioning clutch. The tape tension between the supply reel and the index mechanism is controlled by the adjustment mechanism shown in Fig. 5A-5. This mechanism is designed to keep a very minimal tension on the tape as it enters the index mechanism. If the tape begins to tighten, this raises the paddle. This energizes an air cylinder which engages the drive wheel, advancing the tape.
--[p404]-- The quality of the heat seal is controlled by the temperature, pressure, and dwell time, as the shoes contact the cover tape.
The tape is advanced or indexed by a neoprene pinch roller. This roller has a diameter of seven inches, and is conductive to dissipate any static charges. The roller is driven by a NEMA 23 stepper motor and a micro stepping driver. The drive mechanism has a 100:1 reduction and zero backlash. The finished product is rewound onto an EIA 481 standard reel.
--[p405]-- The drive for the take-up reel includes a magnetic clutch with adjustable tension which is continually overdriven. This provides a consistent take-up tension. The take-up motor is located immediately adjacent to the take-up reel. This motor can be stopped to service or adjust the reel. The nearby switch is labeled "Take-Up On/ Off."

===== [p385-386] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5A.1 INTRODUCTION (3块) =====
--[p385 [表]]-- 5A.1 INTRODUCTION
The tape reel output device can process both embossed and punched carrier tapes. The unit supports all EIA 481 standard tapes, from 8 mm to 32 mm. The unit can also run tapes which range between .25” and 1.00” wide, with sprocket pitches of 0.1”, 0.12” and 0.3.”
The die placed on the tape can range from .020” sq. to 1.00” sq. The indexer can place the die onto the tape with the active side up, or a die inverter can be positioned to place the die with the active side down.
--[p386]-- The vision system can be set up to do post-place- ment inspection. The programmer can change from heat seal to pressure-sensitive cover tapes without changing the tooling. The unit supports cover tape widths from 5.4 mm to 25.5 mm. It can handle supply roll diameters up to 200 mm. The mechanism will accept supply and take-up reels conforming to ElA 481. It can also handle 22”
diameter cardboard supply reels with 3” hubs.
--[p386]-- The changeover to any of the supported carrier tapes can be completed in less than 15 minutes. Most changeovers require no additional tools or tooling.
All components which come into contact with the tape are designed to dissipate static charges. Figures 5A-1 through 5A-6 show the components on the tape reel device. Please refer to these pho- tos as you read this section.

===== [p155-155] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Enable vision (1块) =====
--[p155]-- Enable vision
on the fly When this option is turned on, the arm does not stop to allow the vision system to check the die. The camera checks the die while the arm is moving. Normally this feature is turned on. When this option is off, the arm stops at a $90 ^ { \circ }$ position to allow the vision system to check the die.

===== [p296-303] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5.13 ABOUT DIE INSPECTION (12块) =====
--[p296]-- Instead, an image of each die is presented to the operator, and the operator decides whether to pass the die. The automatic die inspection features are only used with the Auto mode. This is how the system is usually used, and we will describe the Auto mode functions in the rest of this section.
When used on the input side of the machine, these types of inspection occur when the die is still on the wafer. The machine reacts to any damaged or substandard die by skipping or not picking them.
--[p297]-- The person who sets up the inspection program can specify a "fail limit." This sets the number of substandard die the system can find before it stops. If the limit is set to 0, the machine will stop each time it finds a bad die. Once the system does reach the fail limit, the operator can react in three ways:
If the operator thinks that the current die is actually OK, he can press the Continue button. The DS-9000 will pick the current die and continue processing.
--[p298]-- If the operator thinks that the current die actually is bad, he can press the Skip button. The DS-9000 will skip the current die, leaving it on the wafer, and continue from the next die.
If the machine is rejecting too many good die or passing too many bad die, the operator may decide that there is something wrong with the set-up of the inspection system. In this case the operator can retrain the inspection. (To do this, press Done, Tune, and Vision.)
--[p299]-- Let's take a look at the tools or functions which make the die inspection function possible. Each type of inspection, bump or edge chipping, can include several kinds of set-up variables:
A single inspection type may check up to 10 areas or “regions of interest” (ROI’s) on the die. For example, if you were doing bump inspection, you might only want to use one ROI. If you were checking for edge chipping, it might make sense to use four ROI's. See Fig. 5-29.
--[p302]-- Uses input camera (looking downward) or output camera (looking downward)
Usually 1 ROI (see Fig. 5-29)   
(multiple ROI's can be used to avoid problem   
areas in the image)
• Brightness and contrast set to highlight bumps
• Threshold on ROI(s) set to identify bumps
During training, counts the bumps inside the ROI(s)
Adjust tolerance for area variation so system can tell if area occupied by bumps is too large or too small
On input, rejected die not picked, remain on wafer.
--[p298]-- When used on the output side of the machine, the die are inspected before placing. Any die which do not pass inspection are collected by the reject vacuum device.
The die inspection system is very flexible, and can be programmed to cover almost any conceivable situation. This flexibility is possible because many of the variables used in the system are available to the programmer. This means that our explanations in this section of the manual will have to be rather detailed.
--[p301]-- You can specify a white image on a black background, or a black image on a white background.
The "threshold" level used for each inspection type is adjustable. This is the set-point the machine uses to distinguish black from white in the camera image. By adjusting the brightness, contrast, and threshold in combination, you can highlight just the features or problems you are looking for.
Here are summaries of the three kinds of inspection used with this machine:
Bump inspection -
--[p296]-- Bump inspection (checking for number and area of bumps) - can be used on both input and output sides of machine
Edge chipping or edge damage - can be used on both input and output sides of machine
Mark inspection - can be used on both input and output sides of machine
The die inspection system has two operating modes - Manual and Auto. When the system is using the Manual mode, the machine does not attempt to inspect the die.
--[p302]-- On output, rejected die not placed, sent to reject vacuum device
Inside the ROI, the system counts the number of actual bumps, and compares this with the expected number of bumps. The system also checks the area occupied by the bumps, and compares this with the area of the bumps on the original or trained die.
For detailed setup information, see Section 5.14.
Edge damage inspection -
Input camera (looking downward) or output camera (looking downward)
--[p296]-- 5.13 ABOUT DIE INSPECTION
This section explains how to set up the die inspection features on the DS-9000. When this feature is active, the machine checks the die before placing. Inspection can be done by the standard top camera, which is positioned to look down on the input carrier and wafer. Depending on the type of inspection being used, some of the features the system may look for include:
--[p300]-- Each ROI requires a certain amount of processing time, so an inspection type with fewer ROI's will process more quickly.
You can control the lighting used to generate the video image used by the system for each inspection type. To do this, you may specify different gain and offset settings to control the brightness and contrast. All ROI’s used by a single inspection type use the same gain and offset values.
--[p303]-- Usually 4 narrow ROI's - one for each edge (see Fig. 5-29)
Brightness and contrast set to highlight damage
Threshold on ROI's set to inspect the edges of the die
Adjust tolerance for area variation so system can identify edge damage
On input, rejected die not picked, remain on wafer. On output, rejected die not placed, sent to reject vacuum device
For each ROI, the system checks the area occupied by the damage. If this area is larger than the tolerance allowed, the system rejects that die.
For detailed setup information, see Section 5.15.

===== [p484-485] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5A.18 REJECT VACUUM DEVICE (3块) =====
--[p485]-- 5A-20), the entry for Remover Vacuum Dwell refers to the reject vacuum device. This entry sets the duration, in milli-seconds, of the vacuum cycle which removes each die. Use a larger setting for larger die. A typical setting is about 100 mS.
--[p484]-- 5A.18 REJECT VACUUM DEVICE
The reject vacuum device can only be used on a machine with tape reel output, and only for pocketed tape. This mechanism is shown in Fig. 5A-4.
During placing, if a die fails output inspection, a vacuum acts through the reject device to remove the die from the pocket in the tape. A few steps are required to set up the device.
1. The arm at the end of the device must match the size of the die being processed.
--[p484]-- The important measurement is the distance between the barrier blades at the end of the arm. This measurement should be matched to the size of the pocket and die. If the distance between blades is too small, the reject vacuum will not be able to collect the bad die. If the distance is too large, during a reject cycle the reject device may disturb die in adjacent pockets. To change an arm, undo the white Nylon screw at the forward end of the assembly.
2. On the Inverter screen (Fig.

--[p317]-- Training for Bump Inspection
6. Once the bump inspection feature has been turned on, the next job is to set up the parameters used by the inspection system for all of the ROI's included in this inspection. The fields for this are located in the upper left-hand corner of the screen.

12.
--[p319]-- So far, we have been dealing with parameters that apply to all of the ROI's used in this inspection. The next job is to set up the individual ROI(s). Notice the arrow buttons, and the Size and Position buttons in the lower center of the screen. These are used to set the size and position of the ROI. The outline of the ROI will appear on the image presented by the input camera. To set the size of the ROI, click on the Size button, then use the X and Y buttons to change the dimensions.
--[p323]-- The entries for Minimum Size, Maximum Size, and Average Size show the total area of the bumps within the ROI measured in pixels. The entry for Maximum Deviation shows the difference in size between the largest and
smallest bumps in the ROI. The Pass entry shows whether the system thinks this setup will be usable.
The message at the bottom of the screen may indicate that the system did not accept the training setup for this ROI.
In this case the system may suggest a different threshold to use in setting up the gray-scale for this image.
--[p321]-- This tells the system to use the information you have provided to set up the model for the ROI currently being displayed.
15. To check the ROI, click on the Test button. The system will return a score which gives an indication of how reliable the model will be.
16. Normally, you will use only one ROI for bump inspection. You can use more than one ROI when this is necessary to avoid features that will obscure the area to be inspected.
--[p320]-- During input inspection, the camera creates a black-and-white image of the die, and the system analyzes it using an inspection algorithm. For the purposes of the inspection, the algorithm has to see every area of the image as either “white” or “black.” All of the gray areas are identified as one of these two extremes. The setting used for Threshold tells the system where to make the distinction between “white” and “black.”
Start with a value of 32.
--[p318]-- (The value here can range from 0 to 100%.) The smaller the setting for Area Variation, the closer the size of the feature found during inspection has to match the feature on the die used for training.
10. The setting for Color affects the way the image from the camera is presented and interpreted. Choosing the B setting presents an image with black bumps on a white background. Choosing W presents an image with white bumps on a black background.
11.
--[p320]-- The setting for Threshold for an ROI is important because it tells the system how to tell the difference between the black and white areas in the search image. The system uses a grayscale with 64 levels. An area which is pure white is represented by a gray-scale value of 63, and pure black is represented by 0. A value of 32 falls in the middle and represents a medium gray.

===== [p452-463] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Fig. 5A-14 (15块) =====
--[p452]-- Fig. 5A-14
Output Pocket Model Parameters Screen

Table 5A-5 - Output Pocket Model Parameters   
Parameter Description   
Gray Levels This entry specifies the number of grey-scale levels which will be interpreted for the model you are training. The greater the number of grey scale levels, the better the vision system will be able to recognize and interpret details on the die.
--[p454]-- The lowest possible setting is “2.” Using this system, the vision system would recognize every shade of grey as either black or white. As the number of grey levels is increased, this allows the vision system to see more and more intermediate levels of grey in the digitized picture, producing a more accurate repre-
sentation of the model. The possible settings range from 2 to 64. The default entry is “16”, and this is appropriate for most applications.
--[p458]-- The minimum value of “150” results in the search box being one and a half times the size of the model box. If the search box is too large, the computer will present a “Teach Vision” error, and the model will not be saved.
--[p461]-- Look for a sharp image, with good definition between black and white and distinctive features in both the X and Y axes.
5. When you have set all of the parameters for the Pocket model, click on the OK button. The vision system will start to “teach” itself the model you have selected. This teach mode may take a few seconds, depending on the size of the model box and search box.
--[p455]-- Quality Score This item is a measure of how closely the image from the die matches the model. When the two images match, the vision system knows that it has found the correct location on the die. When you are first setting up a model, you “teach” the vision system to recognize a model, and then test the same model. Naturally, the computer returns a high quality figure.
--[p458]-- If the image of the overall die produced by the camera is black with a white border, use the Black setting. If the image is white with a black border, use the White setting.

--[p461]-- The indicates the area of the die where the system is allowed to search for the picture stored in the model box. The search box is a bit larger than the model box, but smaller than the chip box.
Remember that an image which looks good to the human eye may or may not look good to the vision system. When choosing a part of a die to use as a model, look for a part of the image which is unusual or distinctive, and can’t be confused with any other part of the die.
--[p462]-- When the computer is finished, the message “Successfully trained and saved model” will appear in the lower left-hand corner of the screen. If this message does not appear, re-check the size of the search area to confirm that it is does not exceed the viewing area.
6. Before moving on, you should test the model you have just created. Locate the Test button near the jog buttons.
--[p463]-- When you click on this button, the system will analyze the model and return a "vision score" at the bottom of the screen. An acceptable model should have a score of 925 or higher. If the score is lower than this, you will have to improve the model. You can use a different model, change the search area, or change the lighting.
--[p461]-- On the Pocket Model screen, there are several items which allow the programmer to set the way the vision system sees the model and interprets the model information.
At this point in the explanation, we will be discussing three different types of boxes which appear on the screen for the vision system. The chip box follows the outline of the whole die. The model box outlines just the image you want the vision system to recognize.
--[p460]-- 3. Using the Output Pocket Parameters screen, the programmer determines what will be used as a pocket model. Note the buttons labeled Size and Position near the top of the screen. By selecting Size or Position and using the arrow keys, the programmer can size the model box and position the box anywhere on the die.
The programmer can also use the Jog key to centralize the die within the chip box.
4.
--[p457]-- Brightness This changes the brightness of the image shown on the monitor for the vision system. The offset is adjustable from “5” to “255.”
Contrast This adjustment changes the gain or sharpness of the image shown on the monitor for the vision system. The gain setting is adjustable from “5” to “255.”
Box Color The Black setting produces a black-on-white image. The White setting calls for a white-on-black image.
--[p456]-- However, during the run cycle, small differences from one die to another will usually cause the quality scores to be lower.
If the Quality Score is set too high, the system may reject all of the die. If the Quality Score is set too low, the machine may accept a die without correcting for differences along the X and Y axes. For typical alignment applications, the Minimum Quality should range between “600” and “850”, depending on variations in appearance from die to die.
--[p459]-- When setting up the search box for a Rough model only, make the search box as large as possible, while still staying within the viewing screen. This will improve the vision system’s ability to detect
the first die used in the auto alignment sequence.
Search Ratio in Y See the previous item.
Position/Size Training
These allow you to adjust the position and size of the pocket model box.
2. Notice that the chip box should already have been set using the Output Vision Parameters screen (Fig. 5A-13).

===== [p175-179] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5.1 INTRODUCTION (9块) =====
--[p179]-- As part of this process, you choose the alignment method which will be used for this wafer. The three choices are PCM, MRD, or "sparse" alignment.
Next, you show the vision system how to interpret the pictures it receives from the cameras. To do this, you indicate several parts of the die called a “model.” As part of the setup process, you “teach” the vision system how to look for these models.
You test the models, to see that they are working correctly. Finally, you save the setup information.
--[p176]-- As individual die are picked from the wafer, the software needs to know which die are still available. The software records this specific information in the “Map” file. (This type of file has the extension “.MAP.”) if your installation uses a prober to test wafers before they are run, the prober generates the Map file for the wafer. Otherwise, the DS-9000 generates the detailed Map information for each wafer as it is run.
--[p177]-- When you are ready to run a particular job which uses a particular type of wafer, the Laurier software creates a “Job” file. To set up a job, the software looks at the “input” side of the process - the section of the machine where the die are removed from the wafer. To do this, it calls up the generic Wafer file for the type of wafer you want to process. The software adds the Kit information on
the “output” side of the process - the section where the die are delivered.
--[p178]-- The software combines the input and output information, and creates the Job file. (This type of file is identified by the extension “.JOB.”) As the job runs, the system also collects the specific, detailed information on the wafer(s) and includes this in the .MAP file(s). The MAP file(s) also become part of the Job file.
Some installations control the work flow using a system of "Lots." A Lot represents a large run of one particular wafer or product.
--[p177]-- The output section of the machine may be programmed in several different ways. Each of these separate output programs is stored in a "Kit" file (.KIT). For example, let's say that your machine includes a tape reel output, and that you are processing two sizes of die. One output kit would include all of the set-up instructions for the output for one size of die, and another would include separate output instructions for the other size.
--[p175]-- This includes a detailed description of the wafer, including the dimensions of the die, the pitch of the die, and so on. This is a general description of this type of wafer. It can be used as a starting point to describe any wafer of this type. (This information is stored in a file with the extension “.WAF.”) In a moment, we will describe the vision models, which allow the vision system to align with the die on the wafer.
--[p176]-- The Wafer file holds general information about all wafers of a certain type. But as the machine runs the job, it is working with a particular wafer, which may be different from the general type in several ways. Each individual wafer has a “Map” file which stores this specific information. For example, some of the die on a particular wafer may already have been marked as defective, using ink dots.
--[p178]-- When we talk about “Training,” we mean the process of setting up the Wafer file and creating the Kit file and Job file for a particular job. The process of setting up the Wafer file has several steps:
You begin by entering very detailed information about the die on the wafer. Wafer specifications are usually available from engineering data sheets, or from a prober machine. If necessary, you can also get the die specifications by physically measuring the die.
--[p175]-- 5.1 INTRODUCTION
In this section, we will tell you how to set up the Laurier control software so the DS-9000 unit can process a new kind of wafer. In some cases, you can do this by changing an existing product file. In other cases it may be easier to set up a completely new file.
The Laurier software uses several kinds of files to record this product information.:
You begin by setting up a “Wafer” file. This is a generic map of the wafer.

===== [p55-55] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 4.3 ABOUT THE PASSWORDS (2块) =====
--[p55]-- 4.3 ABOUT THE PASSWORDS
This machine may be set up to use a system of passwords. Each password allows access to particular parts of the software. The system used on your machine may include all of the following passwords, or only some of them:
Boot This password allows the user to start the machine.
Tune Parameters Once a job is running, this password allows the user to change some of the machine parameters used in the setup.
--[p55]-- Tune Vision Once the job is running, this password allows the user to change the vision models used in the setup.
Training This password allows the user to set up for a new job, or make modifications, by training machine positions and vision models.
Maintenance This password allows access to the Maintenance functions provided by the software.
Laurier This password should only be used by trained Laurier personnel.
For more information on the password system, see Section 5 - Training.

===== [p159-159] WARRANTY INFORMATION > X inspection error limit (1块) =====
--[p159]-- X inspection error limit
This is the allowable error in the X dimension when using the Output Inspection feature. After placement, the system checks the actual position of the die. If a die is misplaced by more than the tolerance listed here, the machine stops and displays an error.

===== [p196-196] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Multi-Column Offset (1块) =====
--[p196]-- Multi-Column Offset
This entry is used with the entry for Multi-Col Columns. It shifts the column groups horizontally from the center (home) of the wafer by the amount programmed. The setting can range from -3000 to +3000 mils.
Multi-Row Offset This entry is used with the entry for Multi-Row Rows. It shifts the row groups vertically from the center (home) of the wafer by the amount programmed. The setting can range from -3000 to +3000 mils.

--[p182]-- 5.3 COPYING AND EDITING AN EXISTING WAFER FILE
In this section, we’ll assume that you are training the machine for a new wafer which is very similar to an earlier wafer. Rather than enter all of the detailed wafer information, it is easier to copy an older file, make a few changes, then save the file under the name of the new wafer. This will keep the editing to a minimum and allow for quicker programming.
--[p187]-- Open Open an existing LCad file.   
Save Save the current LCad file.   
Print Print a copy of the current LCad image.   
Inspection Get more information about the selected die.   
Pan Move the camera image to another part of the wafer using the mouse or the keyboard. (The wafer does not move.)   
Zoom Zoom in.   
UnZoom Zoom out.   
Add Add the image of a die which really exists on the wafer.   
Delete Remove the image of a die which does not really exist on the wafer.
--[p187]-- Align Identify a die used for alignment.   
Sort Check or change the sort or bin value of a die.   
Jog Move to another part of the wafer by clicking on buttons on the Jog screen. (The wafer actually moves.)   
Reference Die Identify the selected die as a reference die.   
PCM Identify the selected die for use with PCM alignment.   
MRD Identify the selected die for use with MRD alignment.   
Stat In "Status In" the die, making it pickable.   
Stat Out "Status Out" the die, making it unpickable.

===== [p420-420] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Cover Tape Application Position Adjustment (2块) =====
--[p420]-- Cover Tape Application Position Adjustment
The cover tape is guided onto the carrier tape by the position roller. In the case of pressure-sensitive adhesive (PSA) cover tape, sealing occurs at the PSA roller located just downstream. For heat-activated cover tape, sealing occurs immediately after the PSA roller at the heat seal shoes.
By turning the micrometer for the cover tape position adjustment, adjust the cover tape until it is flush with the rear edge of the carrier tape.
--[p420]-- The front side of the cover tape should fully seal the pockets, but not cover any portion of the sprocket holes.
Manually index the tape and adjust the micrometer as necessary.

===== [p446-447] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5A.12 OUTPUT VISION PARAMETERS SETUP (2块) =====

===== [p46-48] Danger! (5块) =====
--[p46]-- Danger!
Failure to follow these rules could cause serious personal injury or death:
Before removing any electrical enclosure covers, remove factory electrical power to the machine. Only certified technicians should perform maintenance or troubleshooting in these enclosures.
A unit may be equipped with an "uninterruptible power supply" which is mounted inside the cabinet (an "internal" U.P.S.). After the Stop button or an Emergency Stop switch is pressed, the U.P.S. will continue to supply
--[p47]-- power to the machine. After two minutes, the U.P.S. will send a signal to power-off the PC and PC monitor. After an additional three minutes, the U.P.S. itself will power off. (To resume operation, press the green Power On button.) An operator should be aware that the U.P.S. may still be supplying power to the machine, even though the Stop or Emergency Stop button has been pressed.
Failure to follow these rules could cause personal injury:
--[p47]-- Do not operate the machine with the covers or guards of any enclosure or mechanism removed. Before operating the machine, be sure to close the clear plastic door. Never operate the machine if the door interlock switches are not working correctly. Do not attempt to reach around the guards or covers for any reason while the machine is running.
The transfer arm safety guard is included as a safety feature for maintenance work.
--[p48]-- When the guard is in the lowered (working) position, the transfer arm operates at the normal speed. When the guard is raised, the arm operates at a pre-set slow speed.
Do not perform maintenance or replacement of pneumatic system parts or pneumatic driven parts without first removing the factory supplied air.
Do not try to adjust or clean any part of the machine while it is running.
Section 8 of the manual includes several mechanical adjustments.
--[p48]-- It is important to make the adjustments in the order presented in Section 8. There are some cases where parts which are out of adjustment may hit or interfere with other parts. By making the adjustments in the correct order, you can avoid possible damage to the machine.

===== [p361-365] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Training for Mark Inspection (7块) =====
--[p363]-- The quality value ranges from “0” to “950”, where “950” indicates a perfect score or match and “0” indicates no match. When the vision system searches for a die, the die shape score must equal or exceed the Quality figure you have programmed. If the score is equal to or higher than the Quality figure, the vision system considers the die to be “found,” and makes the proper corrections.
If the Quality figure is set too high, the system may reject all of the die.
--[p365]-- When you are first setting up a model, you “teach” the vision system to recognize a model, and then test the same model. Naturally, the computer returns a high score. However, during the run cycle, small differences from one die to another will usually cause the quality scores to be lower.

7.
To check the ROI, click on the Test button. The system will return a score which gives an indication of how reliable the model will be.
--[p364]-- If the Quality figure is set too low, the machine may accept a die with an incorrect mark. On a typical mark inspection, the Quality should range between “600” and “850”, depending on variations in appearance from die to die.
The Score figure allows the programmer to see how well the Quality setting is actually working. The Quality figure is a goal, and the Score figure shows the result.

5. The Quality figure is a measure of how closely the image from the die must match the model. When the two images match, the vision system knows that it has found the correct location on the die. The computer judges the success or quality of the match by computing a “shape score” or minimum quality setting.

===== [p313-313] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Area Variation (1块) =====
--[p313]-- Area Variation
The entry for Area Variation determines how closely the feature used for an inspection has to match the original feature on the die you used for training. (The value here can range from 0 to 100%.) The smaller the setting for Area Variation, the closer the size of the feature found during inspection has to match the feature on the die used for training.

===== [p409-410] WARRANTY INFORMATION > When running pocketed tape only - (3块) =====
--[p409]-- When running pocketed tape only -
1. Install the carrier tape supply reel onto the supply shaft as described above.
2. Feed the carrier tape off of the top of the reel in a clockwise direction. Continue to feed the tape around the idler roll and under the small drive roll. See Fig. 5A-5.
3. Feed the carrier tape under the paddle for the tension control device.
4. At this point, the carrier tape is upside-down.
--[p410]-- Feed the carrier tape through the tape guides on the lower right-hand side of the indexer.
5. Slide the carrier tape through the sprocket hole sensor. (The sprocket holes should be toward the front.) Continue to slide the tape through the cover tape rollers and under the heat seal shoes.
6. On the tape index mechanism, lift the drive roller and support it on the cam.
7. Hand wrap the carrier tape onto the take-up reel, then switch on the take-up motor.
--[p410]-- Lift the drive roller motor off of the support cam and lower the drive roller.
8. Position the carrier tape so that the pockets fit down in between the guide plates. Set the tape width adjustment so that the plates are just touching the outside of the pockets, but are not preventing the tape from moving left to right. See Fig. 5A-2. Lock the micrometer in place
and record the setting for future reference.

===== [p109-396] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5. If the cover tape runs out - (4块) =====
--[p396]-- To stop the job, the operator clicks on "N to quit." The computer will ask, "Do you want to make a trailer?" A "trailer" is a section of cover tape which extends beyond the tail end of the carrier tape. Answer "Y" or "N" as required.
To complete the process, the operator presses the Tape Index button to bring the end of the cover tape out of the machine, and then cuts the tape.
For instructions on re-threading the cover tape, see section 5A.4.
--[p109]-- 5. If the cover tape runs out -
Once the tail end of the cover tape passes the sensor, the system will present an error message: "Cover tape has run out." Actually, a few inches of cover tape remain at this point.
If the operator clicks on "Y to continue", the system will place one more die. The operator can repeat this several times if necessary to complete a job which is almost done.
--[p396]-- 5. If the cover tape runs out -

===== [p247-248] WARRANTY INFORMATION > About MRD Alignment (3块) =====
--[p248]-- The system uses this die to make a fine position adjustment. The third or Bottom MRD die is located near the edge of the wafer, on the same Y axis as the first die. The system also checks two more die near the right and left edges of the wafer, as shown in Fig. 5-17.
When the machine is running, the vision system will check the location of each of these die to find the true center position of the wafer, and use this to find the correct location of the first die to be picked.
--[p247]-- About MRD Alignment
If the wafer does not include any special alignment die, the vision system must be set to use MRD (or “Map Reference Device”) alignment. MRD alignment does not require any special alignment die on the wafer.
When the alignment program is set up, the programmer must identify the die which will be used for this type of alignment. To begin this process of identifying the die, the programmer clicks on the MRD button on the LCad screen shown in Fig. 5-3 and 5-4.
--[p247]-- At the start of the process, the machine moves to a die near the center of the wafer. The system refers to this as the Theta MRD die. See the second part of Fig. 5-17. The vision system uses the information from this die to make a rough correction of the position of the wafer. Next, the machine moves to a second die, called the Second Theta die. The die
chosen for this is positioned on the same X axis as the first, at the edge of the wafer.

--[p444]-- Street model - used to locate a corner of the pocket
Ink dot model - used as a backup for the Street model (notice that this is different from the usual function of the Ink Dot model)

• Mark model - used to set up for mark detection
--[p443]-- 5A.11 ABOUT OUTPUT VISION MODELS
If the vision system will be used to inspect the output onto the tape, you must set up the output models used for this. (A "model" is an image of part of the die being placed.) On a unit with this type of output, the system can use three kinds of models to check the alignment for placement. See Fig. 5A-12.
Models Used for Output Vision - Tape Reel Output
Pocket model - after a rejection, used to correct following placements
--[p445]-- The set-up process for an output model is similar to the procedure for any other model, except that Camera 2 (the output camera) is selected instead of Camera 1. Here is a quick summary:
• Set up the size and position of the model box.
• Enter the model parameters for the model.
• Train the system to recognize the model.
• Perform an individual test on the model.
For more detailed information, see Section 5.10.

===== [p250-251] WARRANTY INFORMATION > About Sparse Alignment (3块) =====
--[p250]-- Next, the machine moves to two complete die used to make corrections for any Theta error. (Use relatively larger search areas for these two die.) The machine then goes to a partial die on the side of the wafer, and makes another Theta correction. Next the machine returns to the partial die used for the rough alignment, and rechecks the alignment. Finally, the machine refers to the wafer map to determine which die are still
pickable.
--[p251]-- At this point, the machine is ready to pick the first pickable die.
--[p250]-- About Sparse Alignment
Sparse alignment is used when picking die from a wafer which has already been partially picked. See Fig. 5-18. Since many of the complete die in the center of the wafer may have been picked, this alignment method depends on the partial die around the edge. At the start of the process, the machine moves to a partial die near the far edge of the wafer. The vision system uses the information from this die to make a rough correction of the position of the wafer.

--[p49]-- Caution
Failure to follow these rules could cause damage to the machine:
At the beginning of each shift, operators should confirm that all safety devices are operating properly. They should also check that there are no foreign objects near the moving parts of the machine. Check for tools, loose product, or cleaning materials.
To clean or lubricate any part of the machine, do not use any materials other than those recommended by Laurier Inc.

32. When the correct stroke value is found that corrects for exactly one half the error, re-focus the camera on the top of the inverter.

3. Inspect the leadscrew which moves the table in the X direction. Check for excessive oil on the ballscrew and in the area around the screw. The bulbs used in the illuminator are very hot while they are powered. Allow the bulbs to cool without power for 20 minutes before replacing them.

--[p185]-- Before starting the procedure, confirm that there are no tools, wafers or machine parts which could limit the travel of the input table in either the X or Y directions. Open the access door on the front of the unit. Place an override key on the interlock switch on the front door.

--[p249]-- Move the camera to reference collet 3, and again adjust the front and rear screws to equalize the collets in Y while rotating between collets 3 and 4.
33. At this point the inverter collets are coincident in Y. Verify the collet marks again in the tape, and if collet marks align there, and at the top of the inverter, Y collet coincidence is complete. If the collets do not align in Y at both the top and bottom, some part of this procedure was not followed accurately.
--[p275]-- Caution
Remove the anvil very carefully. As the anvil is moved up and away from the Oring seal, it may come loose suddenly. This can cause the needles to break. Raise the anvil slowly. Do not twist the anvil as you remove it.
--[p287]-- Caution
After changing the anvil home offset, check visually to ensure that the top of the anvil clears the bottom of the input table.
--[p247]-- Caution
Do not move the camera during the next part of the alignment until you are told to do so.
31. Change the inverter flip stroke by a small amount. (A good starting point is 180,030) Index the inverter to @Inspect, place collet 3 down, and again make a collet impression. Index the inverter back to @Place. Repeat the process, adding or subtracting to the inverter flip stroke, until collet 3 down falls accurately on the half way mark.
--[p249]-- Reset the stroke to 180,000 and start over. The collets must be aligned accurately, or the machine will perform poorly.
34. Once the coincidence in Y has been verified, place the inverter in the inspection position with collet 3 up again. Back off the X adjustment
screws until they are loose, then turn them in by hand until they stop. Turn an additional 1/2 turn in to lightly snug the screws.
35. Rotate collet 3 up and down and observe the collet tips.

36. When the camera is aligned to the leftmost collet, rotating the inverter will place the opposite collet slightly to the right. Now adjust the X adjusting screw on this collet to center the collet in the chip box.
--[p215]-- Caution
It is important to make the adjustments in the order presented here. There are some cases where parts which are out of adjustment may hit or interfere with other parts. By making the adjustments in the correct order, you can avoid possible damage to the machine.
1. Before starting, check for the following:
• The power to the machine is on
• The machine has been booted with the Invert option selected
• The home sequence has been completed
2.
While making these adjustments, safety interlocks for the operator and output door must be bypassed for easy access to all axes.
--[p187]-- Apply a small thread of Stazon ballscrew lubricant from the center of the screw about 4 inches from front to back.
8. Inspect the entire table for freedom of motion. Inspect for wear or frayed wires or cable ties. Inspect the area around the home switch for debris.
9. Check the connectors on the motors for the X and Y motions for discoloration or other electrical damage.
10. Check all other connections and sensors for damage or excessive wear.
11. Ensure that the table is free to move.
--[p184]-- Caution
This procedure requires that you override the interlock switch on the front door of the unit. Operating the input table or transfer arm with the door open exposes the operator or technician to potential hazards. Proceed with caution. Do not leave the machine unattended with the operator door open and the override key in place, or with the machine powered up.
1.
--[p185]-- If the screw does not appear wet, apply a small thread of Stazon® ballscrew lubricant (P/N 1013706) from the center of the screw to about 5 inches back.
4. Inspect the lead screw which moves the table in the Y direction. Check for excessive oil on the ballscrew and in the area around the screw as in step 3. Apply a small thread of Stazon ballscrew lubricant from the center of the screw about 5 inches from front to back.
5.

===== [p325-368] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Running Using Top Inspection (9块) =====
--[p325]-- Running Using Top Inspection
1. Once top inspection has been set up and turned on, the machine will run normally. Before each transfer from pick to place, the top or input camera can check each die as it sits in position on the wafer. If the die passes the inspection, the system will place it normally, according to its proper sort assignment. If the die fails inspection, it will be left on the wafer.
2.
--[p368]-- When the machine is running, you can change some of the setup variables for Mark Detection from the Tune Options screen. Select Parameters, then Vision.
You can turn Mark Detection on or off from this screen.
Mark Detection Limits determines the number of inspection failures that must occur before the machine stops and alerts the operator. If the entry for Mark Detection Limits equals 0, the machine does not stop when a die fails Mark Detection.
--[p325]-- If you find that there are too many rejects, or that the system is passing bad die, you can reenter the Training section to change the parameters or the size(s) and location(s) of the
ROI(s). From the Bump Inspection screen (Fig. 5-31), highlight the line for the ROI you want and click on Test. (Do not click on the Train button to reach this screen, or the system will try to re-train one of the inspection models.)
3.
--[p347]-- When the machine is running, you can change some of the setup variables for Edge Damage Detection from the Tune Options screen. Select Parameters, then Vision.
You can turn Edge Damage Detection on or off from this screen.
Edge Chipping Limits determines the number of inspection failures that must occur before the machine stops and alerts the operator. If the entry for Edge Chipping Limits equals 0, the machine does not stop when a die fails Edge Damage Detection.
--[p326]-- When the machine is running, you can change some of the setup variables for Bump Inspection from the Tune Options screen. Select Parameters, then Vision.
You can turn Bump Inspection on or off from this screen.
Maximum Bump Inspection Fails determines the number of inspection failures that must occur before the machine stops and alerts the operator. If the entry for Maximum Bump Inspection Fails equals 0, the machine does not stop when a die fails bump inspection.
--[p367]-- Running Using Top Inspection

If the die passes the inspection, the system will place it normally, according to its proper sort assignment. If the die fails inspection, it will be left on the wafer.
2.
--[p367]-- If you find that there are too many rejects, or that the system is passing bad die, you can reenter the Training section to change the parameters or the size(s) and location(s) of the ROI(s). From the Mark Detection screen (Fig. 5-37), highlight the line for the ROI you want and click on Test. (Do not click on the Train button to reach this screen, or the system will try to re-train one of the inspection models.)

===== [p68-68] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Maps? (1块) =====
--[p68]-- Maps?
The .MAP files include the specific information about the die in this lot. If you click on Yes, the computer will make a "fresh start" with the wafer(s) in the lot, assuming that all of them are new and unpicked. If all of the die have been picked, or if the wafers will never be used for any other lot, you can click on Yes to delete the .MAP files. If you click on No, the computer will work with any existing information it has about the wafer(s) in the lot.
If you are working with wafer(s) which have been partially picked, click on No to save the .MAP files.

===== [p38-38] Loader arm - (1块) =====
--[p38]-- Loader arm -
After a wafer frame is positioned at the correct height by the elevator, the loader arm grips the wafer frame. The loader arm then pulls the wafer frame back, and places the wafer on the input table. When a wafer has been picked completely, the loader arm unloads the wafer and places it back in the cassette.

===== [p397-399] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5A.3 THREADING INSTRUCTIONS - CARRIER TAPE (2块) =====
--[p397]-- 5A.3 THREADING INSTRUCTIONS - CARRIER TAPE
Figure 5A-7 shows the correct threading path for the carrier tape. Figure 5A-5 shows the supply and take-up reels.
1. Use the Cover Tape Up/Down switch to raise the PSA ("pressure-sensitive adhesive") roller.
2. Lift the drive roller and support it in the raised position. The motor for the drive roller can be held in this position by a support cam, shown in Fig. 5A-3.
3.
--[p399]-- Attach the new carrier tape supply reel to the hub at the bottom of the feed/take-up unit.
4. Thread the tape along this route:
• over the idler roll
• under the tape unit
• around the nose roll
• under the pocket sensor
• under the four hold-down springs
• under the PSA roller
• under the drive roller
• up to the take-up reel
5. Use the Cover Tape Up/Down switch to lower the PSA roller.
6. Press the Tape Index button a few times to bring the carrier tape out of the machine.
7.
Re-start the existing job.

===== [p202-203] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5.4 SETTING THE DIE PITCH (4块) =====
--[p202]-- The table will move so that the physical center of the die is directly under the camera. Move to a location 10 die to one side along the X axis. The table should move so that the center of that die is directly under the camera. The outline of the chip box should exactly match the outline of the die.
If the die appears to be a bit out of position along the X axis, this tells you that the pitch setting on the X axis is not quite right.
--[p202]-- 5.4 SETTING THE DIE PITCH
1. The next step is to check the pitch (the distance between centers) of the die on the wafer. You should also check the number of die in the largest row (on the X axis) and the longest column (along the Y axis) on the wafer. You can do this from the LCad screen shown in Fig. 5- 3. Click on the Align icon from the column of icons to the right of the LCad screen, then click on a die in the center of the wafer.
--[p203]-- You will have to decide whether the pitch needs to be increased or decreased. Click back to the center die, then click to another die which is located two or three die to the left along the X axis. If the image of the die appears to the left of the chip box, then the pitch should be in-. This means that the table is traveling farther than necessary. If the die is to the right of the chip box, then the pitch should be de-. Go back to the Wafer/Waffle Parameters screen (Fig.
--[p203]-- 5-6) and change the setting for Chip X Width. Repeat this procedure until you can move back and forth over 10 die without seeing any change in the position of the image of the die. Repeat the same procedure for the Y axis as well.
When you enter a new pitch setting, the computer will ask you to verify the change.

===== [p5-5] 1.2 PARTS IDENTIFICATION (1块) =====
--[p5]-- 1.2 PARTS IDENTIFICATION
Most machine parts discussed in this manual are shown schematically and/or pictorially in the illustrations which are listed by drawing number in the drawings included with this manual. ===== [p245-246] WARRANTY INFORMATION > About PCM Alignment (4块) =====
--[p246]-- Finally, the machine moves to a third die, referred to as the Second Theta die. This is located on the same axis as the other Theta die, but sits at the edge of the wafer.
--[p246]-- A PCM die can be placed anywhere on the wafer, but the one used for this purpose is located at the center of the wafer. By analyzing the position of this PCM die, the vision system makes initial corrections to the position of the wafer. Next, the machine moves to another die called the First Theta die. The die chosen for this is positioned beside the PCM die. The vision system checks this second die, and makes a finer adjustment to the position of the wafer.
--[p245]-- About PCM Alignment
The DS-9000 unit may be set up to use three kinds of vision processing. When the wafer includes one or more special test alignment die, it is possible to use PCM (or “Process Control Module”) alignment. PCM alignment is preferred because it is faster.
When the alignment program is set up, the programmer must identify the die which will be used for this type of alignment.
--[p245]-- To begin this process of identifying the die, the programmer clicks on the PCM button on the LCad screen shown in Fig. 5-3 and 5-4.
To set up for this kind of alignment, the trainer identifies three die on the wafer. See the first part of
Fig. 5-17. At the beginning of the process, the machine moves to a special PCM die. The PCM die is used only to help in positioning the wafer. It does not include any functional parts, and is never picked by the machine.

You can use any number between 0 and 500 to identify this cassette.
