===== [p236-238] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Stop At Reference Die (4块) =====
--[p236]-- Stop At Reference Die
Before picking, the machine can be programmed to stop at a designated "reference die" and present an image of the die on the vision system. The operator can then approve, or can use the jog buttons to correct the location. This provides a double-
check to ensure that the machine has found the correct location on the wafer.
Street Correction “Street” correction refers to a final step in the alignment process.
--[p238]-- Street Interval This item determines how often the machine will do a street correction. If the entry here is “1”, the machine will do a street correction for each individual die. (This is the recommended setting if you are teaching a new wafer.) A setting of “1” is also recommended if Ink Dot Recognition is turned on. If this entry is set to “5”, street correction will only be performed for every fifth die. The allowable range is 1 - 100.
--[p237]-- Most of the steps in input alignment are concerned with correcting the position of the whole wafer. Street correction is used to correct the position of each individual die. The entry on this line turns the feature on or off. (Also see the entries for Street Interval and Maximum Street Fails below.)
Auto Retry Street The system can be set to try the Street alignment a second time on the same die before declaring an error.
--[p238]-- Street Fail Limit This determines how many consecutive failures of street alignment the system will tolerate before it triggers an error. If the entry here is “1”, the machine will stop if a single street correction is unsuccessful. (This is the recom-
mended setting if you are teaching a new wafer.)
Enable Ink Dot Die which are incomplete or should not be picked may be marked using ink dots. When this feature is turned on, the machine can reject any marked die automatically.
Ink Model This entry determines the type of model used to set up the ink dot feature.

===== [p52-52] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 4.1 INTRODUCTION (2块) =====
--[p52]-- 4.1 INTRODUCTION
This section includes operating instructions for the DS-9000 unit. As you operate the machine, the control software will present a series of screens and prompts. These will lead you through the operating sequence. In this section, we will describe most of the screen items available to the operator, and explain how they work.
Besi/Laurier recommends that anyone who operates the machine have a basic understanding of the “Windows XP®” operating system.
--[p52]-- If you are not familiar with Windows XP, see the tutorial located in the Program Manager under the Help menu. This tutorial explains the basic keystroke and mouse functions required to operate in a Windows environment.

===== [p147-150] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Tape reel: (5块) =====
--[p148]-- From this position, the needle can react quickly when it is actually time to pick the die. The Ready stroke is the distance, in Tmils, from the Offset position to the Ready position.
The Mid-Stroke position provides a mid-point during the peel. The Mid-Stroke stroke is the distance, in Tmils, from the Ready position to the Mid-Stroke position.
As the final part of the cycle, the needle moves up to the Full Stroke position, and the die is separated from the film and picked up by the collet.

Tune - Vision Parameters
--[p149]-- On a cassette-to-cassette machine (with film frame output), the Placement Compensation feature can be used to make corrections if the die are consistently being placed to one side of the real or desired placement position. See Fig. 4-28.

The DS-9000 may be supplied with an output system which can place die in waffle trays or gel packs. This mechanism includes an X - Y output table or stage similar to the table on the input side of the machine. The output table can index the pockets in the waffle tray below the output side of the transfer arm. This allows the transfer arm to place die in each pocket in turn. The system can handle $2 "$ waffle trays or gel packs in an array of 12.

--[p233]-- 5.9 SETUP FOR AUTO ALIGNMENT
The next step in wafer programming is to determine what type of vision correction to use for this wafer. Each wafer is mounted slightly differently on its frame. This means that, when the machine moves a wafer from the elevator onto the input table, the wafer may not be perfectly aligned with the table. The center of the wafer may be slightly out of position along the X or Y axes. The whole wafer may also be rotated or turned a bit, requiring a Theta correction.

===== [p11-11] 1.5 BACKUP PROCEDURES (2块) =====
--[p11]-- 1.5 BACKUP PROCEDURES
Several different types of files used by this system should be backed up regularly.
.WAF Each .WAF file is a description of one type of input wafer, including information on die size, etc. The information in the .WAF file is "generic" because the file describes the wafer before picking.
.MAP As the machine picks die from the input wafer, it stores an updated map of the wafer. The .MAP file for each wafer shows which die have been picked, and which die remain.
--[p11]-- .KIT Each .KIT file includes information on a specific setup for the output side of the machine.
.JOB Each .JOB file represents a complete setup for the machine. The .JOB file connects the generic information about the input wafer (.WAF file) with the output setup (.KIT file).
All of the files listed above are stored in the Laurier directory on the hard drive on the machine. For more information on all of these file types, please refer to Section 5 - Training.

This sets the limit of the area checked by the vision system. It should exactly match the outline of the die as it is presented on the monitor for the vision system.

--[p304]-- Mark inspection -
This is similar to the ink dot inspection, except that the system is looking for an irregular mark rather than a dot. The mark used could include one or more printed characters. The system does not read the characters, but is checking for the correct shape.

===== [p267-269] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5.12 VISION TRAINING (6块) =====
--[p268]-- In this part of the manual, we will concentrate on the models used on the input side of the machine.
You do not have to teach the models in any set order. Just remember to teach all of the models needed for each setup.
When choosing vision models, it is important to find an area of the die which cannot be confused with other parts of the die. Look for unusual or unique features on the die.
--[p268]-- During the alignment process, the Pattern Recognition System uses several different models to check the die on each wafer:
The first or Rough model is used when the vision system first “captures” or finds the center of the wafer.
The second and third models are called Right Theta and Left Theta models. The vision system uses these two models to correct the Theta or rotational position of the wafer.
--[p267]-- 5.12 VISION TRAINING
In the last step, you told the vision system where to for the die used for alignment. Now, you must show the system how to interpret the die images. Before, you were concerned with the positions of the die used in alignment. Now you want to help the vision system to identify a particular part of the die image.
--[p269]-- This is especially important for the Street model, since this can be used to check each die which is picked. (The programmer can decide how often the vision system will check the Street model and make an X-Y correction.)
Here is the process used to program the vision system to interpret the die images:
1. From the Input Training screen (Fig. 5-3), click on the line for Rough Model. The computer will present a screen similar to Fig. 5-23. Table 5-5 explains the items on this screen.
--[p267]-- To make accurate placements during the pick and place cycle, the DS-9000 uses a “Pattern Recognition System.” This captures an image of the die, and compares it with another image or “model” which has already been stored. When the actual image matches the stored model, the computer knows it has found a certain location on the die. This allows the system to adjust for any inaccuracy in the wafer, and also determine whether the die is acceptable for picking.
--[p268]-- The fourth or Street model is used during the run cycle to check all or most of the individual die on the wafer.
The machine can be set up to check each die before picking. For more information on input die inspection, see Section 5.13.
As the die are placed, the system can be set to refer to several different kinds of output models. Depending on the type of output, the machine can used these models in different ways. For more information, see Section 5A, 5B, or 5C.

Enter the setting on the temperature control for the front heat shoe.

===== [p121-121] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 4.11 EMERGENCY STOP - INTERNAL U.P.S. (2块) =====
--[p121]-- 1. The E-Stop switch will cut off power to the motors, and will stop all pneumatic functions. The computer which supervises the machine will continue to run. The computer will automatically shut down the machine in an orderly way, and close all files. This means that, when the machine is restarted, it should not skip over any die.
--[p121]-- 4.11 EMERGENCY STOP - INTERNAL U.P.S.
Some units have an "uninterruptible power supply" which is mounted inside the cabinet (an "internal" U.P.S.) On this type of machine, you can use any of the Emergency Stop switches to stop the machine quickly in an emergency. There are three of these switches. (The red Stop switch on the operator’s panel acts as an Emergency Stop.) Once you press any of the E-Stop switches, an error message will appear, and the red indicator on top of the machine will light.

===== [p414-414] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Carrier Tape Input Tension Adjustment (2块) =====
--[p414]-- Carrier Tape Input Tension Adjustment
The tape tension between the supply reel and the index mechanism is controlled by the adjustment mechanism shown in Fig. 5A-5. This mechanism is designed to keep a very minimal tension on the tape as it enters the index mechanism. If the tape begins to tighten, this raises the paddle. This energizes an air cylinder which engages the drive wheel, advancing the tape.
--[p414]-- The additional length of tape reduces the tension, allowing the paddle to lower, and de-energizing the air cylinder.
1. As the paddle is lifted, a flag at the hub of the paddle assembly trips a sensor. This produces a signal which activates the air cylinder.
2. If necessary, the tension can be adjusted by moving a flag on the shaft of the paddle slightly. To increase the tension, raise the flag.

--[p159]-- Output inspection

===== [p240-240] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Enable Vision on Fly (1块) =====
--[p240]-- Enable Vision on Fly
When this option is turned on, the arm does not stop to allow the vision system to check the die. The camera checks the die while the arm is moving. Normally this feature is turned on. When this option is off, the arm stops at a 90° position to allow the vision system to check the die.
VOF Delay This sets a delay period, in milliseconds, from the moment the arm starts moving until the point where the vision system can begin checking the die.
This time delay allows the arm to move out of the way of the camera. ("Enable Vision on Fly" must be turned on - see item above.)

===== [p7-10] 1.4 SOFTWARE ARRANGEMENT (6块) =====
--[p9 [表]]-- The machine uses the same keyboard and monitor to work with both of the computers - the main computer running LMAIN and the second computer running the NetSight software. When the machine is first started, the keyboard and monitor will automati-
cally be set to control the main computer. You can switch back and forth between the two computers using this procedure: Keyboard and monitor control machine operation (main computer) - Press Control, and then &quot;1.&quot;
--[p7]-- Some of the tabs or buttons in LMAIN start or call up other areas or sections of the software.
LCAD From LMAIN, clicking on the Train button brings you to LCAD. LCAD can be used when the machine is running to display a graphic image of the wafer being set up or picked. LCAD can also be started and run separately. Because this can sometimes cause conflicts in the machine, you should not start LCAD separately while the machine is running.
--[p8]-- It presents real-time information on the inputs and outputs active on the machine.
NetSight This software controls the action of the vision system. The vision software is used to inspect the die being picked on the input side and the die being placed on the output side of the machine. The NetSight software runs on a second computer which is connected to the main computer running LMAIN.
--[p7]-- 1.4 SOFTWARE ARRANGEMENT
The complete system includes several different sections or areas of software. These sections work together in various ways to provide all of the functions on the machine. Each section runs as a separate program or application using the Windows operating system. Several of these programs may be running at the same time.
LMAIN This is the main program which supervises the operation of the machine. LMAIN controls startup, pick and place functions, and shutdown.
--[p8]-- Toolbox From LMAIN, clicking on the Maintenance button brings you to the Toolbox. This function allows you to move most of the mechanisms or axes on the machine.
MIS This utility can be reached via a button in LMAIN. The MIS program is a separate "Management Information System" program. It collects statistical information on the performance of the machine.
IO Status This utility can be reached via a button in LMAIN.
--[p10]-- Keyboard and monitor control NetSight computer - Press Control, and then &quot;2.&quot; The control infor- mation will appear on the monitor for the vision system. To reset both the main computer and the computer

===== [p416-416] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Optional Inverter Set-Up Procedure (2块) =====
--[p416]-- Whenever the machine prepares to run a job which requires the inverter, the inverter will move into position automatically. When the machine shifts to a job which does not require the inverter, the inverter will automatically retract.
--[p416]-- Optional Inverter Set-Up Procedure
The DS-9000 can accept die with the active side up. (This is how they are traditionally delivered.) The unit can place die in the same way, with the active side up. The unit also includes a die inverting assembly which can be positioned to place the die active side down. Figure 5A-4 shows the inverter assembly.
During the setup process the programmer can specify use of the inverter as part of the job setup.

===== [p492-494] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5B.4 ABOUT DIE POSITIONS DURING OUTPUT (2块) =====
--[p494]-- The individual waffle trays or gel packs can be placed on the output table in any orientation. Part C of the figure shows one possibility, but the waffle trays could be placed in other orientations. In the example shown here, pin 1 on the die is located closest to the notched corner of the waffle tray or gel pack.
There is no formal rule to set this orientation. The machine can place die in any orientation desired. Just keep this issue in mind as you set up the output table.
--[p492]-- 5B.4 ABOUT DIE POSITIONS DURING OUTPUT
As you make the physical setup of the output table, you must consider the final position the die should take in the waffle tray or gel pack. The simplest way to explain this is by keeping track of the position of pin 1 on the die. See Fig. 5B-3.
Let's say that, at the beginning of the process, the die sits on the wafer in the position shown in part A of Fig. 5B-3. After picking and placing, the transfer arm rotates the die 180°, as shown in part B.

===== [p2-2] 1.1 INTRODUCTION (1块) =====
--[p2]-- 1.1 INTRODUCTION
The Laurier DS-9000 is a high-speed, fully-automatic pick and place system for die sorting applications. In numerous installations throughout the IC industry, the DS-9000 has significantly reduced user costs through higher yields, increase productivity, and unmatched system reliability.
This manual includes instructional and reference material for the operator, programmer, and maintenance technician who works with the DS-9000.
Here is a quick summary of the sections of this manual:

===== [p244-244] WARRANTY INFORMATION > On a machine with optional programmable lighting - (1块) =====
--[p244]-- On a machine with optional programmable lighting -
This entry may be used to control the intensity of the light source directly. (The dial on the light source itself must be set to the "P" position to allow for programmable control.)

===== [p137-137] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Needle dwell (1块) =====
--[p137]-- Needle dwell
This sets the length of time, in milli-seconds, that the needles remain up after the Collet Down Dwell period. (This entry also controls the Collet Up function.)

2. If you want to make an adjustment or remove something inside the machine, click on Pause, then Door. The screen shown in Fig. 4-1 will appear.
3. Some of the setup parameters can be changed or “tuned” while the machine is running. Use the Tune button at the bottom of the Runtime Scoreboard screen. For details, see Section 4.12.

===== [p3-3] Section 4 - Operation Instructions (1块) =====

===== [p144-144] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Post inverter down/ vacuum on/off (1块) =====
--[p144]-- Post inverter down/ vacuum on/off
Normally, the vacuum for the inverter is turned on before the inverter collet is lowered. If the machine is handling a type of die which includes bumps, the bumps may cause the collet on the transfer arm may have a weaker grip on the die. In a case like this, the inverter may take the die away from the collet on the transfer arm too quickly. When this feature is turned on, the
vacuum for the inverter is not turned on until the die has been placed by the collet on the transfer arm.

===== [p417-417] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Cover Tape Supply Reel Tension Adjustment (1块) =====
--[p417]-- Cover Tape Supply Reel Tension Adjustment
The tension of the cover tape supply reel should be set to the minimum force that will keep the cover tape taut. The cover tape between the supply reel and the carrier tape should not sag. Wide pressure sensitive (PSA) tapes will need more tension than thin ones. Too much tension may result in buckling or mis-indexing.
1. To make a tension adjustment, loosen the knurled knob on the tensioning brake.
While turning the supply shaft slowly (in either direction), adjust the graduated ring.
2. When the setting is correct, tighten the knurled knob.

This shows whether the machine will check for a distinctive pattern or "mark" before picking or placing each die. See Section 5.16.

===== [p210-210] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Multi Column Offset (1块) =====
--[p210]-- Multi Column Offset
This is used in conjunction with Multi-Col Pitch. This entry horizontally shifts the column groups in the generic map from the center (home) of the wafer by the amount programmed. The setting can range from -3000 to +3000 mils. A positive number offsets to the right of the map.

===== [p428-428] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Place Collet Puff Dwell (1块) =====
--[p428]-- Place Collet Puff Dwell

===== [p40-40] Input table - (1块) =====
--[p40]-- Input table -
As the machine operates, the wafer frames are placed on this table by the loader arm. This table can move very precisely in the X and Y directions. The top of the table can also turn or rotate to correct for any mis-alignment of a wafer. (This is called “Theta correction.”) The top of the table includes a “stretcher” mechanism. Once a wafer is in place, the stretcher plate clamps down to stretch the wafer so the die can be removed easily.

===== [p138-138] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Post pick delay (1块) =====
--[p138]-- Post pick delay
Normally, during the pick cycle, the collet is down and the needle is up for the same length of time. By adding a value for post pick delay, it is possible to keep the needle up a bit longer. In some situations this helps the die to separate from the tape. See the figure below.

===== [p146-146] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Reject limit (1块) =====
--[p146]-- Reject limit
When the reject vacuum device is active, if a die fails inspection, the vacuum will collect the bad die. The machine will then try to place another die in the same pocket. After the number of failures set by the reject limit, the machine will stop and call for operator action.

===== [p419-419] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Cover Tape Position Roller (2块) =====
--[p419]-- Cover Tape Position Roller
The roller is designed to guide the cover tape precisely onto the carrier tape. Two kinds of stepped guide rollers are available.
1. Choose the roller which includes a step which matches the tape width. One type of roller has steps for 12 and 24 mm. The other roller includes steps for 8, 16, and 32 mm.
2. Hold the D-ring of the release pin and press the button. Slide the axle pin through the currently-installed roller and the mounting bracket.
--[p419]-- Remove the existing roller and replace it with the new one.
3. While still pressing the button, slide the pin through the mounting bracket and the roller. Release the button. The roller is now locked in place.

===== [p349-349] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5.16 MARK INSPECTION (1块) =====
--[p349]-- 5.16 MARK INSPECTION
Mark inspection is similar to ink dot inspection, except that in this case the system is looking for an irregular mark rather than a dot. The mark used for this could be any distinct high-contrast shape, and could include one or more printed characters. The system does not read the characters or use an optical character recognition process, but checks for the correct shape or pattern.

===== [p369-372] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5.17 OUTPUT TRAINING (3块) =====
--[p372]-- The upper left-hand side of the screen lists the existing output files. The list on the lower left shows the parameters which can be adjusted to set up a working output arrangement. Notice that three of these items refer to a specific type of output device - in this example, the tape and reel output. For more information on setting up the type of output device on your machine, please check the material in Section 5A, 5B or 5C.
--[p369]-- 5.17 OUTPUT TRAINING
So far, we have been working with the input side of the machine. This is the area which includes the of the die used in the process. On the output side, the machine places the die in a container or device which is the final product or the destinaof the process.
The DS-9000 can use different output placement routines, depending on the output options purchased with the system. The unit offers three output options:
• Tape and reel system See Section 5A
--[p369]-- • Waffle tray or gel pack See Section 5B
• Film frame See Section 5C
In the sections noted above, you will find set-up instructions for the output device(s) included with your machine. When you are finished working with the main part of Section 5, please refer to the appropriate section for the type of output included on your machine.
1. When you select Training, and then Output, the system presents the screen shown in Fig. 5-38.
2.

===== [p424-425] WARRANTY INFORMATION > Take-up Reel Tension Adjustment (2块) =====
--[p425]-- To stop the take-up motor to service or adjust the reel, turn off the nearby switch labeled "Take-Up On/Off."
2. Remove the cover. This is held in place with two # 10 cap screws. Loosen the knurled knob on the tensioning brake. While turning the supply shaft slowly, adjust the graduated ring. (You can turn the supply shaft in either direction.) Tighten the knurled knob.
3. Replace the cover and switch the motor back on.
--[p424]-- Take-up Reel Tension Adjustment
The take-up reel is driven by a magnetic clutch with adjustable tension. This clutch is continually
overdriven to provide a consistent take-up tension on the carrier tape. The take-up motor is located immediately adjacent to the take-up reel. The amount of tension required will vary with product; wide or thick tapes will need more tension than thin ones.
1.

===== [p243-243] WARRANTY INFORMATION > On a machine with optional programmable zoom - (1块) =====
--[p243]-- On a machine with optional programmable zoom -

===== [p400-400] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 5A.4 THREADING INSTRUCTIONS - COVER TAPE (2块) =====
--[p400]-- 5A.4 THREADING INSTRUCTIONS - COVER TAPE
Figure 5A-8 shows the correct threading path for the cover tape. Figures 5A-1 and 5A-3 identify the parts of the tape reel mechanism.
1. Use the Cover Tape Up/Down switch to raise the PSA ("pressure-sensitive adhesive") roller.
2. Attach the new cover tape supply reel to the hub on top of the tape reel unit.
3. Thread the tape along this route:
• around the outside of the peel-off arm.
• through the tape-out sensor
--[p400]-- • around the outside of the vertical roller
• over the upper horizontal roller
• under the positioning roller
• under the PSA roller
4. Use the Cover Tape Up/Down switch to lower the PSA roller.
5. Press the Tape Index button a few times to bring the cover tape out of the machine.
6. Re-start the existing job.

The programmer can set up an inspection process to ensure that the die are being placed correctly. The entry here determines how often the machine will stop and ask the operator to check the die at the output. At the start of a job, it is typical to check every 1,000 or 5,000 die.

===== [p421-421] WARRANTY INFORMATION > For cover tape with pressure-sensitive adhesive - (1块) =====
--[p421]-- For cover tape with pressure-sensitive adhesive -
The cover tape is pressed onto the carrier tape by the PSA roller. When the product is de-reeled, the peel force depends on the amount of pressure used when the cover tape is sealed.
Use the Cover Tape Pressure air regulator to change the sealing pressure. (This regulator is located on the upper front control panel. See Fig. 5A-6.) Typically the pressure is set to 30 to 40 PSI.

Configuring Input and Output

2.
--[p112]-- Once the waffle tray or gel pack output has been set up, it should operate with very little operator attention until all die on the wafers have been picked, or until the all of the waffle trays or gel packs on the carrier are full.

When you click on "Y" to continue, the machine will continue to place die. To stop the job, click on "N to quit."

===== [p107-107] WARRANTY INFORMATION > 3. Ifthe output tape reaches the programmed number of die - (2块) =====
--[p107]-- Answer Yes or No as required.
To complete the process, the operator presses the Tape Index button to bring the end of the carrier tape out of the machine, and then cuts the tape.
For instructions on re-threading the carrier tape, see section 5A.3.
--[p107]-- 3. Ifthe output tape reaches the programmed number of die -
As it operates, the DS-9000 keeps track of the number of die placed on the output reel. When the software determines that the correct number of die have been placed, a status message will appear on the screen: "Pocketed tape is full."
To stop the job, the operator clicks on "N to quit." The computer will ask, "Do you want to make a trailer?" A "trailer" is a section of cover tape which extends beyond the tail end of the carrier tape.

===== [p421-422] WARRANTY INFORMATION > For cover tape with heat-activated adhesive - (2块) =====
--[p421]-- For cover tape with heat-activated adhesive -
A pair of heat seal shoes are positioned downstream of the cover tape positioning roller. At programmable intervals, the shoes are pneumatically actuated. On each side of the tape, a heated copper shoe is driven down onto the cover tape, sealing it to the pocketed tape. The quality of the heat seal is controlled by the temperature, pressure, and dwell time.
1. The dwell time for the heat seal depends on the type of cover tape being used.
--[p422]-- This is detailed in the product-training menu.
2. The temperature is controlled independently for the front and rear shoes. For most kinds of heat-activated cover tape, the operating temperature should be $1 8 0 ^ { \circ } C$ to $200 \%$ . Use the up- and down- arrows on the controllers to select the desired temperature.

Parameter Description
Input:
Street correction
interval This item determines how often the machine will do a street correction. If the entry here is "1," the machine will do a street correction for each individual die. Offset This sets the initial position of the first frame, measured in TMils.

===== [p142-142] WARRANTY INFORMATION > For waffle tray or film frame output- (1块) =====

When the Placement Compensation feature is turned on, the output table can make adjustments to make the placement action more accurate. This is helpful when working with very small die. See step 6 in this section.

--[p295]-- Note
Notice that the X and Y adjustment screws have opposite effects on the position of the head. Turning the X adjustment screw clockwise "pushes" the head away from the screw. Turning the Y adjustment screw clockwise "pulls" the head towards the screw. See Fig. 8-45.
To make a change in the X or Y position, begin by loosening one of the two adjusting screws. Change the position of the head using the other screw, then tighten the first screw.

===== [p188-188] 7.7 STRETCHER (2块) =====
--[p188]-- Inspect the four pneumatic cylinders for the stretcher for signs of damage or excessive looseness of the air fittings. Also inspect the air hoses for signs of damage and leaks. Replace any questionable components.
4. Move the stretcher up and down using the Relays menu. Check that all four corners of the stretcher move equally in both directions.
5. If the motion on one corner is not equal, then locate the four air flow controls mounted on the left side of the table, under the X-Y plate.
Adjust the appropriate controls until the motion is equal at all four corners. Lock each adjustment screw by tightening the jam nut with a wrench.
--[p188]-- 7.7 STRETCHER
1. Before starting the procedure, confirm that there are no tools, wafers or machine parts which could limit the travel of the input table in either the X or Y directions. Open the access door on the front of the unit. Go to the Tool Box icon.
2. Home the input table, and then disable motion by opening both front doors. Check that the stretcher is down, and the transfer arm is at the park position.

===== [p49-67] Search Ratio in X (8块) =====
--[p67]-- Search Ratio in Y See the previous item.
Position/Size Training
These allow you to adjust the position and size of the pocket model box.
1. For this type of output, the Ink Dot model is used as a backup for the Street model to locate a second corner of the pocket. (Set up the Ink Dot model on the corner of the pocket opposite to the corner used for the Street model. See Fig. 5B-10 and 5B-17.
--[p67]-- Don’t make the search box so large that it cannot be viewed on the monitor for the vision system. If the search box is too large, the computer will present a “Teach Vision” error, and the model will not be saved.
When setting up the search box for a Rough model only, make the search box as large as possible, while still staying within the viewing screen. This will improve the vision system’s ability to detect the first die used in the auto alignment sequence.
--[p66]-- Search Ratio in X
The search box controls the size of the area where the vision system will search for the model. The entry for Search Box Ratio sets the size of the search box in relation to the size of the model box. This value is a percentage of the model box size. The minimum value of “150” results in the search box being one and a half times the size of the model box. An entry of “200” results in a
search box which is two times the size of the model box.
--[p50]-- Don’t make the search box so large that it cannot be viewed on the monitor for the vision system. If the search box is too large, the computer will present a “Teach Vision” error, and the model will not be saved.
When setting up the search box for a Rough model only, make the search box as large as possible, while still staying within the viewing screen. This will improve the vision system’s ability to detect the first die used in the auto alignment sequence.

The alignment for the loading and unloading of wafers is now complete. We recommend that several wafers be cycled through the input using the Cycle Wafers option. If any problems occur, repeat the procedure.

===== [p287-290] 8.11 ALIGNING COLLETS TO CAMERAS (5块) =====
--[p289]-- It may be necessary to jog the Input table so that the Teflon tape is visible through the input camera. Select Input XY Table from the Motor Axes menu. Use the N,S,E and W buttons in the Jog menu to position the tape.
6. Select Anvil from the Motor Axes menu. Click on the Home button to position the anvil, and then click on the Vac On box located in the Jog menu. Place a .010” shim centered on the anvil.
--[p287]-- 8.11 ALIGNING COLLETS TO CAMERAS
This procedure is used to align the collet positions to the input camera, and to also check the alignment of the two collets to each other. This is accomplished by making impressions of the collets on a strip of Teflon® tape and comparing the marks.
1. Place a strip of Teflon tape onto a film frame using wafer mounting tape.
2. Select Input from the Motor Axes menu.
--[p288]-- Click on the Cycle Wafer Button located in the upper right hand corner of the screen, and the screen shown Fig. 8-42 will be displayed.
3. Click on the XY Table to Load button. The input table will move to the load position, and the stretcher assembly will open. Place the film frame with the piece of Teflon tape into the input table stretcher assembly.
4. Click on the XY Table to Pick button, and the table will return the pick position. Click on the OK button.
5.
--[p290]-- This will move collet 2 over the input area and the Teflon tape.
--[p289]-- (The shim is used to eliminate any interference that the vacuum holes or rings may cause when the collet is making a mark in the Teflon tape.) Click on the Stroke Up button, located in the Jog menu, to place the anvil at the normal pick height.
7. Select Transfer Arm from the Motor Axes menu, and the screen shown in Fig. 8-43 will be displayed. Click on the Home button to ensure the Transfer Arm is at the correct position, and then click on 0 in the Jog menu.

===== [p285-294] Toolbox Screen (9块) =====
--[p286]-- If the anvil is within acceptable tolerances, this procedure is complete - skip to Section 8.12.
5. Subtract 2250 from the position displayed in the Current Position area. Click on the Motor Parameters tab. At the next screen, select Anvil from the Choose an Axis to Edit menu. Adjust the offset by the calculated amount, then click on the Set Parameters button.
6. Click on the Toolbox tab. Select Anvil from the Motor Axes menu, click on Home, and repeat steps 3 and 4.
--[p292]-- It may be necessary to center the collet impression into the square chip box. This is done by adjusting the alignment screws on the camera mount. This adjustment should only be
done with collet 2. After aligning collet 2 to the input camera, make several impressions to ensure the accuracy of the alignment. Always use a fresh section of tape before making another mark.

--[p286]-- Jog the anvil up using the Up button, located in the Jog menu, until the anvil deflects the bezel of the gauge to 0. This will place the top of the anvil even with the top of the stretch ring.
4. Record the anvil position displayed in the Current Position area. This should be at 2250. Variances of up to +5 are acceptable. If the anvil is not at a motor position of 2250, the anvil offset should be adjusted.
--[p285]-- Toolbox Screen
2. Place the setup gauge on the input table so that the contact point will make contact with the center of the anvil. It may be necessary to jog the input table to center the gauge to the anvil. (It is not possible to jog the input table with the gauge on the stretch ring, as it will be interrupting the stretcher up safety sensor.) --[p293]-- Jog the input to a fresh section of Teflon tape using the N, S, W, and E buttons located in the Jog menu.

--[p293]-- This will enable viewing of the impression made by collet 1. If the mark is not centered to the chip box, an adjustment of some kind will be necessary. Continue with this procedure. If the collet marks are both centered to the chip box, go to section 8.13.

--[p291]-- Toolbox Screen
8. Click on the In Collet box located in the Jog menu. Use the Down button to jog collet 2 into the Teflon tape. Over drive the collet approximately .030.” This will make a visible impression in the Teflon tape. Do not touch any part of the transfer arm. This may cause inaccuracies in the impression and misalignment to the camera. Record the In Collet position displayed in the Current Position menu.

--[p292]-- At the next screen, click on In Collet, located in the Choose an Axis to Edit menu. Click on the Stroke field, and enter the recorded position. Click on the Set Parameter button, and then click on the Toolbox tab.
10. Select Transfer Arm from the Motor Axes menu. Click on the Up button located in the Jog menu, and then Click on 90. This will raise collet 2 and move the Transfer Arm to enable you to see the collet impression on the tape.

===== [p192-192] 8.1 INTRODUCTION (1块) =====
--[p192]-- 8.1 INTRODUCTION
In this part of the manual, we will include two kinds of information.
The first part of this section includes information on some utility functions provided by the Laurier software. These allow a set-up person to move and adjust various parts of the machine.
The following paragraphs provide information on mechanical adjustments for the DS-9000 unit. These adjustments should be done in the order presented in this section.

--[p343]-- The lower trip point should have an offset of 2 to 5 from this point, so in this case:
533 + 2 = 535 (for lower trip point)
The upper trip point should be about 5 unit above this, so:
535 (lower trip point) + 5 =
540 (for upper trip point)

14.
--[p342]-- Setting Trip Points
12. In the following steps you will be setting the “trip points” for the sensor to allow it to detect the presence of a die. When the vacuum falls within these trip points, the system knows that there is a die on the collet. The machine uses this information to detect pick or mispick conditions. We are only concerned with settings P-1A and P-2A. (The P-3A and P-4A settings are for output 2, and are not used).
--[p346]-- In our example we could raise each setting by 3:
540 (upper trip point) + 3 =   
543 (new upper trip point)   
535 (lower trip point) + 3 =   
538 (new lower trip point)
Enter the new numbers and test the action again.
17. When the OUT1 LED transitions on and off cleanly, move to the next collet. Repeat the trip point setup for the associated pressure switch.
This completes the setup of the SMC pressure switches for the collets on the transfer arm.
--[p361]-- It is important to set the trip points carefully because they have a large impact on the machine throughput (UPH). If the trip points are set too high, the machine throughput will drop dramatically. If the trip points are set too low, the machine will not recognize a missed die and will leave an empty pocket in the tape. The machine will then slow down to perform a missing die replacement.
In the tests we just ran, the value with the collet unblocked was 533.
--[p361]-- Setting Trip Points
12. In the following steps you will be setting the “trip points” for the sensor to allow it to detect the presence of a die. When the vacuum falls within these trip points, the system knows that there is a die on the collet. The machine uses this information to detect pick or mispick conditions. We are only concerned with settings P-1A and P-2A. (The P-3A and P-4A settings are for output 2, and are not used).
--[p342]-- It is important to set the trip points carefully because they have a large impact on the machine throughput (UPH). If the trip points are set too high, the machine throughput will drop dramatically. If the trip points are set too low, the machine will not recognize a missed die and will leave an empty pocket in the tape. The machine will then slow down to perform a missing die replacement.
In the tests we just ran, the value with the collet unblocked was 533.

===== [p178-179] 7.1 MAINTENANCE SCHEDULE (3块) =====
--[p179]-- Wafer Elevator | Clean tracks. | 7.8 | Once every threemonths, or once for each10,000 wafersprocessed.
Vacuum Generators | Clean filters | 7.9 | Once every six months
--[p179]-- General Cleanup | 7.2 | Once a week
Wafer Loader | Clean Roh&#x27;lix slide | 7.3 | Once a week
Collet Inspection | Check rubber tips andinspect shanks | 7.4 | Once a week
Optics for VisionSystem | Clean glass surfaces,replace bulbs | 7.5 | Once a month
Input Table | Oil ballscrews, inspectwire-ways. | 7.6 | Once a month, or oncefor each 1,000,000 dieprocessed
Stretcher | Check hoses andfittings for leaks. | 7.7 | Once a month, or oncefor each 4,000 wafersprocessed.
--[p178 [表]]-- 7.1 MAINTENANCE SCHEDULE
This section describes the preventive maintenance schedule which should be followed for the DS-9000 unit. The user should understand the operation and maintenance procedures for the unit, and also the Windows XP® operating system, before following the procedures listed here. Many of the parts of the machine are called out in the photos in Section 1. Section 8 includes details on the adjustments listed here.
System Component | Operations Performed | SeeSect. | Frequency

===== [p39-39] Inspection Fail Alarm Count (1块) =====
--[p39]-- Inspection Fail Alarm Count
After a certain number of inspection failures, the machine can be set to pause and alert the operator. If this is set to zero, this feature is turned off.

===== [p304-306] tor. Center the anvil to the input camera using the micrometers on the anvil assembly. (3块) =====
--[p306]-- Click on Set Parameters to save the setting.
6. Click on Input Y located in the Choose an Axis to Edit menu. Click on the Offset field and modify the current offset by the recorded Y position. Click on Set Parameters to save the setting. Click on the Tool Box tab to return to Tool Box.
7. Repeat Steps 1-3 and check the centering of the input table and anvil.
--[p304]-- tor. Center the anvil to the input camera using the micrometers on the anvil assembly.
Tool Box Screen
3. Using a steel rule, or some other precision measurement tool, measure the distance from the center of the anvil to the left, right, upper and lower sides of the stretch ring. See Fig. 8- 49. If the anvil is not centered, jog the input table to center the table around the anvil. See the next step. If the anvil is centered, skip to Section 8.13.
Centering Anvil
4.
--[p305]-- If it is necessary to center the table, select Input from the Motor Axes screen, and then select XY Table. Jog the input table using the N, S, W, and E buttons in the Jog menu. The distances from side to side and front to back should be within .020."
5. Record the X and Y positions displayed in the Current Position area. Click on the Motor Parameters tab. In the Choose an Axis to Edit menu, click on Input X. Click on the Offset field and modify the current offset by the recorded X position.

===== [p213-213] Miscellaneous Screen (1块) =====
--[p213]-- Miscellaneous Screen
You can reach this screen by clicking on Maintenance, and then Miscellaneous. The following screen is shown in Fig. 8-7.
This screen includes two utility functions. The file "Paramdb" includes many of the unique setup parameters for a particular machine. Normally, this file should only be altered by a trained Laurier technician. The second item provides a way of testing the bar code reader used with the optional cassette elevator.

===== [p69-69] 5B.15 OTHER KINDS OF OUTPUT DIE INSPECTION (2块) =====
--[p69]-- 5B.15 OTHER KINDS OF OUTPUT DIE INSPECTION
So far, we have been using the vision system to control the placement of the die on the output side of the machine. The NetSight vision system can also do three kinds of die inspection:
• Bump inspection
• Edge damage detection
• Mark inspection
We described the setup for each type of inspection in Section 5 of the manual.
--[p69]-- Since the setup procedure is almost the same for the input and output version of each inspection, we will not repeat all of the instructions here. For more information, please check these sections:

===== [p2-20] Pocket Row Pitch (10块) =====
--[p20]-- Green Next place position in the busy direction
Yellow Next place position in the lazy direction
8. The system needs to be able to find each of the die placement locations on the gel pack. You enter this information by "training" the system to go to some key locations on the gel pack.
The entry for Select Waffle to Train shows which gel pack is being programmed at the moment. Start by specifying gel pack #1 (called waffle tray #1).
--[p19]-- It is a good idea to begin placing die on the far side of the gel pack which is farthest from the input table. As the transfer arm places additional die, it will not have to move above die which have already been placed. This reduces the chances of disturbing the die in the gel packs.
The software uses color codes to indicate the different die as they appear on the screen. The default colors are:
Red First place position on the whole array
Blue First place position on a particular gel pack
--[p17]-- Pocket Size Y This is the size of each pocket measured in the Y direction.
6. When editing is complete, click on the Set Parameters button. The LCAD software will create a drawing of the waffle tray array as shown in Fig. 5B-4.
7. Notice the area labeled Waffle Pack Training in the lower left corner of the screen. Lets take a moment to explain some of the entries in this area. The entry for "first" refers to the location of the first die to be placed in the gel pack.
--[p4]-- The entry for "first" refers to the location of the first die to be placed in a waffle tray. In order to understand the entries for "busy" and lazy," See Fig. 5B-6. There are two ways the machine can place die in a waffle tray. The machine will make the most frequent motions in the direction chosen as "busy." Once a particular row or column is full, the machine will shift to the next row or column.
--[p6]-- The default colors are:
Red First place position on the whole array
Blue First place position on a particular waffle tray
Green Next place position in the busy direction
Yellow Next place position in the lazy direction
8. The system needs to be able to find each of the pockets or die locations on the waffle tray. You enter this information by "training" the system to go to some key locations on the waffle tray.
The entry for Select Waffle to Train shows which waffle tray is being programmed at the moment. Start by specifying waffle tray #1.
--[p2]-- Pocket Row Pitch
On any one of the waffle trays, this is the space between corresponding points on the waffle trays (i.e. upper left-hand corner of one pocket to upper left-hand corner of next pocket) measured in the Y direction.
Pocket Column Pitch
On any one of the waffle trays, this is the space between corresponding points on the waffle trays (i.e. upper left-hand corner of one pocket to upper left-hand corner) measured in the X direction.
--[p4]-- Because this shift to the next row or column occurs less often, this is identified as the "lazy" direction.
It is a good idea to begin placing die on the far side of the waffle tray which is farthest from the input table. As the transfer arm places additional die, it will not have to move above die which have already been placed. This reduces the chances of disturbing the die in the waffle trays.
The software uses color codes to indicate the different die as they appear on the screen.
--[p16]-- Pocket Row Pitch
On any one of the gel packs, this is the space between corresponding points on the gel packs (i.e. upper left-hand corner of one pocket to upper left-hand corner) measured in the Y direction.
Pocket Column Pitch
On any one of the gel packs, this is the space between corresponding points on the gel packs (i.e. upper left-hand corner of one pocket to upper left-hand corner) measured in the X direction.
Pocket Size X This is the size of each pocket measured in the X direction.
--[p3]-- Pocket Size X This is the size of each pocket measured in the X direction.
Pocket Size Y This is the size of each pocket measured in the Y direction.
6. When editing is complete, click on the Set Parameters button. The LCAD software will create a drawing of the waffle tray array as shown in Fig. 5B-4.
7. Notice the area labeled Waffle Tray Training in the lower left corner of the screen. Lets take a moment to explain some of the entries in this area.
--[p18]-- In order to understand the entries for "busy" and lazy," See Fig. 5B-7. There are two ways the machine can place die in a gel pack. The machine will make the most frequent motions in the direction chosen as "busy." Once a particular row or column is full, the machine will shift to the next row or column. Because this shift to the next row or column occurs less often, this is identified as the "lazy" direction.

===== [p178-178] SECTION 7 MAINTENANCE (1块) =====
--[p178]-- SECTION 7 MAINTENANCE

===== [p179-180] 7.2 GENERAL CLEANUP (2块) =====
--[p179]-- 7.2 GENERAL CLEANUP
1. Open the front access door of the machine. Confirm that there are no tools, wafers or machine parts loaded on the input table that could limit the table travel in either the X or Y directions. Also confirm that the compressed air for the system is turned on, and that the regulator is set for 75 p.s.i. Vacuum the top surface of the baseplate. Remove any loose die or other loose items.
2. Wipe the outer surface of the machine with water.
--[p180]-- If a solvent is needed, use dilute ethanol. Clean the Lexan® windows and the two monitor screens using a commercial glass cleaner.
3. Go to the Tool Box icon. The machine may ask you to enter a password. Enter the password which allows access to the Maintenance level of the software. Check to be sure that all parts of the machine (or all “axes”) are free to move. Check the input table, transfer arm, and below the elevator. Close the Lexan door and press the green Start switch.
Send all of the parts of the machine to their home positions by clicking on Home All Axes in the Tool Box menu.

===== [p51-54] Position/Size Training (6块) =====
--[p53]-- The search box is a bit larger than the model box, but smaller than the chip box. When setting up the pocket model, the search box should be a bit larger than the pocket. Set the size of the search box using the Search Ratio in X and Search Ratio in Y controls.
5. When you have set all of the parameters for the Pocket model, click on the OK button. The vision system will start to “teach” itself the model you have selected.
--[p53]-- This teach mode may take a few seconds, depending on the size of the model box and search box. When the computer is finished, the message “Successfully trained and saved model” will appear in the lower left-hand corner of the screen. If this message does not appear, re-check the size of the search area to confirm that it is does not exceed the viewing area.
6. Before moving on, you should test the model you have just created. Locate the Test button near the jog buttons.
--[p54]-- When you click on this button, the system will analyze the model and return a "vision score" at the bottom of the screen. An acceptable model should have a score of 925 or higher. If the score is lower than this, you will have to improve the model. You can use a different model, change the search area, or change the lighting.
--[p52]-- At this point in the explanation, we will be discussing three different types of boxes which appear on the screen for the vision system. See Fig. 5B-13.
Boxes Used for Output Pocket Model
The chip box follows the outline of the whole die. The model box outlines just the image you want the vision system to recognize - in this case, the corner of the pocket. The indicates the area of the die where the system is allowed to search for the picture stored in the model box.
--[p51]-- By selecting Size or Position and using the arrow keys, the programmer can size the model box and position the box anywhere on the die. The programmer can also use the Jog key to center the die within the chip box.
4. On the Pocket Model screen, there are several items which allow the programmer to set the way the vision system sees the model and interprets the model information.
--[p51]-- Position/Size Training
These allow you to adjust the position and size of the pocket model box.
2. Notice that the chip box should already have been set using the Output Vision Parameters screen (Fig. 5B-11).
3. Using the Output Pocket Parameters screen, the programmer determines what features on the die will be used as a pocket model. Note the buttons labeled Size and Position near the center of the screen.

===== [p332-332] Table 8-2 - Controls on Pressure Switch (1块) =====
--[p332]-- Table 8-2 - Controls on Pressure Switch
Key Function
Up Increases the setting of a numerical value
R (Reset) Clears the display or clears an error
A (Auto) Auto Preset mode - not used
S (Set) Changes the input mode
Down Decreases the setting of a numerical value. This also changes the measurement units or the output mode.

===== [p73-73] Inverter Lead Time (1块) =====
--[p73]-- Inverter Lead Time
When the die is transferred from the transfer arm to the inverter, the collet on the transfer arm lowers. The inverter should begin moving down a moment before the transfer arm collet moves.
This reduces the impact between the collet on the transfer arm and the inverter. The variable entered here sets the length of the "head start" given to the inverter, measured in milli-seconds.
Post Inverter Down/Vacuum On

===== [p193-273] Warning (12块) =====
--[p216]-- Warning
During normal operations, the safety interlocks for the doors should never be bypassed. The operator should never have to do this. There are some special maintenance and adjustment procedures which do require the interlocks to be bypassed. Do not work on the machine when it is in this condition unless you are a trained technician. Always restore the interlock system and ensure that it is working correctly before releasing the machine for service.
3.
--[p273]-- Warning
The needles are very sharp, and can be damaged easily. To avoid injury, handle them carefully. Always grip a needle assembly by the base.
--[p217]-- The person(s) performing the setup will require the following tools:
• 1 - set of feeler gauges
• 1 - standard screwdriver (small head)
• 1 - roll of Teflon® tape
• 1 - 3” adjustable wrench
• 1 - set of ignition wrenches
• 1 - set of standard Allen wrenches
• 1 - Setup gauge P/N 9036061
It is also helpful to have a complete set of other tools.
--[p193]-- Warning
It is important to make the adjustments in the order presented here. There are some cases where parts which are out of adjustment may hit or interfere with other parts. By making the adjustments in the correct order, you can avoid possible damage to the machine.
Please read Section 4 of this manual (“Operating Instructions”) before performing the mechanical adjustments. Some of these adjustments may require the use of passwords.
--[p205]-- On the Motor Parameters screen, the entries for motor speeds are listed in motor steps per second. Initial and Slew velocities are independent variables. Ramp parameters are computed internally depending on specified speeds, ramp, stop, and
selected step resolution. Step motions are controlled at speeds up to 23,000 steps per second. The screens for all of the stepper motors are identical.
--[p232]-- Warning
Do not allow the inverter to home into the transfer collets.
15. Using a straight edge or small precision square, verify that the inverter head is vertical. If it is not, jog the head until it is vertical. Using the Motor Parameters screen, modify the inverter rotate offset as necessary. Re home and verify that the inverter is vertical. When the inverter head homes correctly, move to the next step.
16.
Because you are performing maintenance/ alignment of the inverter head, you will use the inverter X motor offsets to align collet tips.
--[p194]-- Warning
In some of the mechanical adjustment procedures, we recommend that the power to the machine be turned off for safety reasons. Before doing this, always “close” any job which is being run on the DS-9000. As the software closes a job, it records the point in the process where the machine was stopped. This allows the machine to continue to complete the job when it is restarted.
There may be a few cases where you will want to leave the power on during an adjustment.
--[p193]-- The software can be set up to include three categories of passwords. These allow access to the Run, Maintenance, and Training sections of the software. To perform all of the adjustments listed in this section, you will have to know the passwords for the Maintenance and Training functions.
Note that the machine may use several combinations of vacuum collets, needles and anvil assemblies. These allow the machine to handle die of different sizes.
--[p195]-- If the machine is in the middle of a production run, and the user is familiar with the adjustment required, then the power may be left on. This will prevent any disruption of the machine cycle. If you do this, always install a Machine Down tag to prevent anyone from starting or using the machine while adjustments are being made.
--[p205]-- Warning
If you replace a sensor, you may need to adjust the Stroke and Offset variables. Do not adjust any of the other parameters on this screen without approval from a Laurier field service engineer. If you enter an incorrect value, this could prevent the machine from running correctly. It could also result in serious damage to the system, and will terminate the warranty. Most readers will never have to change any of these parameters, and can probably skip the rest of this section.
--[p253]-- Warning
Push the collet all of the way into the top of the collet holder before tightening the set screw. If you do not do this, the collet may hit the stretcher when the machine operates. This may break the collet cylinder.
5. Turn the arm so that collet 2 is near the front of the machine. Remove the interlock key and close the operator door. In the Door menu, click on Done. This will home the arm.
--[p194]-- For discussion purposes, the following procedures assume that the anvil has four holes, the needle assembly has four needles, and the vacuum collet is a single point. The adjustment procedures for other combinations of anvil, needle and vacuum collet are similar.

===== [p181-182] 7.3 WAFER LOADER (3块) =====
--[p181]-- With the loader arm back, wipe the Roh’lix® slide forward of the moving bearing block with a clean dry cloth. Use a small amount of ethanol on the cloth as a solvent. Use no oil of any kind.
5. Pull the loader arm forward until it contacts the input table. Wipe the Roh’lix slide behind the moving bearing block with the same cloth.
6. Push the loader back to its rear limit.
7. Ensure that the table and stretcher is free to move. Enable motion by closing the left front door.
--[p182]-- Press the green Start button. Perform a Home All. Jog the loader forward and home the loader assembly several times. Check that the loader can move freely. Check for normal operation of the entire input assembly.
--[p181]-- 7.3 WAFER LOADER
1. Before starting the procedure, confirm that there are no tools, wafers or machine parts which could limit the travel of the parts of the machine. Go to the Tool Box icon. Remove the input cassette if it is mounted in the elevator.
2. Home the input table. Click the boxes for Cycle Frames and XY Table to Load to position the input table at the loading position.
3. Disable motion on the machine by opening the front doors. Check that the stretcher is up.
4.

===== [p201-203] Input/Output Status (2块) =====
--[p201]-- Input/Output Status
The Status screen shows a real-time display of all of the input and output signals to the control computer. See Fig. 8-4. The inputs are generated by proximity switches at various points on the machine. As a particular switch is opened and closed, you can see the display for that item change from Off to On. This display can be very helpful when
you are debugging problems where a sensor, valve or, motor controller signal could be causing a system malfunction.
--[p203]-- Notice that each input or output has a different “address,” identified by a combination of board, connector, port and bit. These addresses also appear on the wiring diagrams and schematics. Figure 8-4 shows just one of several possible screens. To see other parts of the complete display, use the slider labeled Next/Previous Port. You can also go directly to a particular item using the menus for Select a Port and Select a Signal.

===== [p339-359] Testing Collet Performance (4块) =====
--[p340]-- Vacuum On - Collet Unblocked
11. Next, block the collet tip with a die or a small piece of paper. (Do not use your fingers - they will contaminate the tip of the collet. This contamination can cause die release problems.) The display should rise to a high value (well over 600) as the vacuum increases. See Fig. 8-67.
Vacuum On - Collet Blocked
--[p359]-- Vacuum On - Collet Unblocked
11. Next, block the collet tip with a die or a small piece of paper. (Do not use your fingers - they will contaminate the tip of the collet. This contamination can cause die release problems.) The display should rise to a high value (well over 600) as the vacuum increases. See Fig. 8-81.
Vacuum On - Collet Blocked
--[p339]-- Testing Collet Performance
10. With the collets inserted, turn on the vacuum to the machine and observe the display. The value that appears will depend on several factors. Primarily, the diameter of the vacuum passage in the collet sets this value. The smaller the passage (hole), the higher the value of this number. In this example, turning on the vacuum produced the dis-
play shown in Fig. 8-66. The value which appears on your machine will vary depending upon the size of the collet hole.
--[p358]-- Testing Collet Performance
10. With the collets inserted, turn on the vacuum to the machine and observe the display. The value that appears will depend on several factors. Primarily, the diameter of the vacuum passage in the collet sets this value. The smaller the passage (hole), the higher the value of this number. In this example, turning on the vacuum produced the display shown in
Fig. 8-80. The value which appears on your machine will vary depending upon the size of the collet hole.

--[p93]-- Major Start Side This parameter allows the pro- | grammer to designate where the input will start picking, and along
with Major Direction, which way the system will drive while pick- ing. See Fig. 5C-8. Major Direction This parameter, along with Major Start Side, enables the programmer to start on any side of the wafer and

===== [p279-283] 8.9 ADJUSTING NEEDLE OFFSET (6块) =====
--[p282]-- Click on the Motor Parameters tab, then in click on Needle in the Choose an Axis to Edit menu. Adjust the needle offset by the calculated amount. The offset cannot be changed to a negative amount. If the adjustment makes the offset into a negative amount, it is necessary to adjust the needle home sensor down and redo the needle offset procedure starting at step 1.
8. Click on the Set Parameters button, and then click on the Toolbox tab.
9.
--[p282]-- Note the current position of the needle displayed in the Current Position area. This should be between 50 and 70. If this is correct, then the setup procedure is now complete and you can skip to step 9.
6. If the needle position is not between 50 and 70, then it is necessary to make an adjustment to the needle offset. To calculate how much the needle needs to be adjusted, subtract 50 from the displayed position.
7.
--[p280]-- If the gauge moves, then the Needle is already above the surface of the anvil and must be lowered.
4. Use the Up and Down buttons, located at the Jog menu, to position the needles so that they are just below or even with the top surface of the anvil. See Fig. 8-40. The best way to check for this is to jog the needles until the gauge barely deflects, and then jog the needle down until the gauge returns to the original 0 position.
5.
--[p279]-- (It is not possible to jog the input table with the gauge on the stretch ring, as it will be interrupting the stretcher up safety sensor.) Remove the gauge, jog the input table, and replace the setup gauge.
2. Select Needle from the Motor Axes menu, and the screen shown in Fig. 8-39 will be displayed.
Tool Box Screen
3. Click on the Home button. Watch for any movement of the gauge while homing the needle.
--[p279]-- 8.9 ADJUSTING NEEDLE OFFSET
This procedure is used to set the correct initial needle height. The setting will place the tips of the needle(s) .005” below the top surface of the anvil insert.
1. Place the setup gauge on the input table so that the contact point will make contact with the center of one of the needles. If necessary, jog the input table to center the gauge to one of the needles.
--[p283]-- Check the needle setting by selecting Needle from the Motor Axes menu, and then click on the Home button. Recheck the needle height as described in step 3. Home the needle again when finished to return the needle to the Home position.

--[p349]-- (On a tape reel machine, the collets should also be installed on the inverter.) If it is not clear what type of collets to install for your application, call the factory for instructions.
2. Figure 8-72 shows the front panel of a pressure switch. Table 8-3 lists the controls on this switch.
--[p349]-- If the collet is holding a die, the pressure switch measures a drop in air pressure, and confirms that the die is present. Each pressure switch must be programmed with the correct pressure set-points. See the procedure which follows.
1. Select appropriate collets for the die to be picked. These collets should be installed in the pick/place collet holders.
--[p348]-- 8.16 ADJUSTING THE DIE DETECTION SENSORS - OPTIONAL INVERTER
The machine needs a way of confirming that an inverter collet has actually picked up a die. The pressure switches handle this job. The switches for the two collets on the inverter are mounted to the left of the inverter mechanism. See Fig. 8-71.
When picking and placing, the machine provides a vacuum to the hole at the center of the collet.

===== [p254-259] 8.7 TRANSFER ARM COLLET HEIGHT ADJUSTMENT (5块) =====
--[p255]-- Click on the Training Menu from the Main screen. This will access a pull down menu. Click on Input. The system will present the screen shown in Fig. 8-31.
3. The screen includes a list of existing products in the Input Wafer Files menu, in the upper left corner of the screen. Double click on "11SETUP." When the product is loaded, the product file name will be displayed at the top of the screen.
4. To load the Lighting and Zoom settings, click on the Wafer/Waffle Vision menu.
--[p256]-- This menu is located under the Input Recipe List menu. (The menu can be found directly under the Input Wafer Files.) The screen shown in Fig. 8-32 will appear.
Input Vision Parameters Screen
5. Click on the Chip Box button, and the screen shown in Fig. 8-32 will be displayed.
6. You will notice the Lighting and Zoom settings adjust to the programmed settings. If necessary, these settings can be modified before exiting the menu.
7.
--[p258]-- Once the desired Lighting and Zoom settings are loaded, exit the Input Training window.
Click Done, Done, and then Exit. If the Save window is displayed, click on Yes to save the desired changes. Click on the Maintenance button at the bottom of the screen to return to the Maintenance screen.
8. If you are using rubber collets, remove the tips from the collet shanks. Turn the Collet Z Force Pressure down to 0.
9. Check for freedom of movement in collet 2.
--[p259]-- Position collet 2 toward the front of the machine by selecting Transfer Arm from the Motor Axes Menu. The screen shown in Fig. 8-33 will be displayed.
--[p254]-- 8.7 TRANSFER ARM COLLET HEIGHT ADJUSTMENT
This procedure ensures that collets 1 and 2 are being lowered to the same level during the pick and place process. This is accomplished by measuring the collet heights using a specific stroke and making contact with a setup gauge.
1. Start by setting up the machine so you are able to view the collet impressions made on the input side. Click on the Laurier Main button at the bottom of the screen to switch windows.
2.

===== [p203-204] Motor Parameters (2块) =====
--[p204]-- 8-5.
--[p203]-- Motor Parameters
Many of the mechanical functions on the machine are controlled by stepper motors. (Some of the functions are pneumatic.) The Motor Parameters function is used to change or adjust the behavior of all of the main functions except the transfer arm. Using Motor Parameters, you can adjust motor speeds, strokes and offsets.
After you click on Maintenance and Motor Parameters, the machine presents a screen which allows you to choose which mechanical function you want to edit. See Fig.

===== [p273-273] Removing the Anvil (1块) =====
--[p273]-- Removing the Anvil
1. Press the green Start switch to boot the machine.
2. After the system boots up (approximately 1.5 minutes), the unit will prepare to home all of the axes. Check to see that there are no “extra” parts or objects inside the machine which might block movement of the parts of the machine. Click on the box labeled OK using the left-hand button on the mouse. All axes will move to the home positions.

===== [p207-210] Table 8-1 - Edit Motor Parameters (6块) =====
--[p209 [表]]-- Run Current | This sets the percentage of cur- rent applied to the motor while an axis is in motion.
Max. Velocity Acceleration | This sets the final slew velocity after the motor ramps up to speed in the middle of a move.
This entry determines how many steps are made at each step rate point on the acceleration/decel- eration curve during ramping. Using higher numbers will greatly increase the time of the accelera-
tion/deceleration ramp.
--[p208 [表]]-- Using a value of “2” or “3” may be helpful in producing smoother acceleration at slower speeds.
Enable This entry determines the way the axis interprets the limit switch function.
Trip Point Not used
Slow Jog | This determines the number of motor steps per second during a slow jog function.
Hold Current | This sets the percentage of cur- rent applied to the motor while an axis is in a stationary position.
--[p207]-- Using higher numbers will greatly increase the time of the accelera- tion/deceleration ramp. For maxi- mum acceleration/deceleration, use lower numbers.
Step Resolution This selects the step size resolution. An entry of “1” specifies half step resolution or steps of .0005”. An entry of “0” specifies a full step resolution, or steps of .001”. This should always be set to “1.”
Divider All speeds during ramping and slewing are divided by the specified number.

Initial Velocity | This sets the initial motor speed in steps per second. This vari- able is used at the beginning and
Slew Velocity | the end of a move. This sets the final slew velocity after the motor ramps up to speed in the middle of a move.
Ramp | This entry determines how many steps are made at each step rate point on the acceleration/decel- eration curve during ramping.

Inspection Interval
The programmer can set up an inspection process to ensure that the die are being placed correctly. The entry here determines how often the machine will stop and ask the operator to check the die at the output. At the start of a job, it is typical to check every 1,000 or 5,000 die.

===== [p55-58] 5B.13 OUTPUT STREET MODEL SETUP (5块) =====
--[p57]-- When you are first setting up a model, you “teach” the vision system to recognize a model, and then test the same model. Naturally, the computer returns a high quality figure. However, during the run cycle, small differences from one die to another will usually cause the quality scores to be lower.
If the Quality Score is set too high, the system may reject all of the die. If the Quality Score is set too low, the machine may accept
--[p58]-- Box Color The Black setting produces a black-on-white image. The White setting calls for a white-on-black image. If the image of the overall die produced by the camera is black with a white border, use the Black setting. If the image is white with a black border, use the White setting.
--[p58]-- For typical alignment applications, the Minimum Quality should range between “600” and “850”, depending on variations in appearance from die to die.
Brightness This changes the brightness of the image shown on the monitor for the vision system. The offset is adjustable from “5” to “255.”
Contrast This adjustment changes the gain or sharpness of the image shown on the monitor for the vision system. The gain setting is adjustable from “5” to “255.”
--[p55 [表]]-- 5B.13 OUTPUT STREET MODEL SETUP
1. When you select Output, and then Street Model, the system will present the screen shown in Fig. 5B-14 and Table 5B-7.
Table 5B-7 - Output Street Model Parameters
Parameter | Description
Quality Score | This item is a measure of how closely the image from the die matches the model. When the two images match, the vision sys- tem knows that it has found the correct location on the die.
--[p56]-- The computer judges the success or quality of the match by computing a “shape score” or minimum qual- ity setting. The quality value ranges from “0” to “950”, where “950” indicates a perfect score or match and “0” indicates no match. When the vision system searches for an die, the die
have programmed. If the score is equal to or higher than the Quality Score figure, the vision system considers the die to be “found,” and makes the proper corrections

===== [p330-331] 8.15 ADJUSTING THE DIE DETECTION SENSORS TRANSFER ARM COLLETS (3块) =====
--[p330]-- 8.15 ADJUSTING THE DIE DETECTION SENSORS TRANSFER ARM COLLETS
During the pick cycle, the machine needs a way of confirming that a collet has actually picked up a die. The pressure switches handle this job. The switches for the two collets on the transfer arm are mounted above the arm. During picking, the machine provides a vacuum to the hole at the center of the collet. If the collet is holding a die, the pressure switch measures a drop in air pressure, and confirms that the die is present.
--[p330]-- Each pressure switch must be programmed with the correct pressure setpoints. See the procedure which follows.
1. Select appropriate collets for the die to be picked. These collets should be installed in the pick/place collet holders. (On a tape reel machine, the collets should also be installed on
the inverter.) If it is not clear what type of collets to install for your application, call the factory for instructions.
2. Figure 8-58 shows the front panel of a pressure switch.
--[p331]-- Table 8-2 lists the controls on this switch.

===== [p211-211] Actions (2块) =====
--[p211]-- Actions
The DS-9000 uses relays to control a series of air valves, and these air valves control various machine functions. The Actions screen allows a technician to test all of these air valves. When you click on Maintenance and then Actions, this opens the display shown in Fig. 8-6. This screen can be used to set the collet height, move the loader, and activate other pneumatically-controlled functions. Each time you click on one of the boxes, the controller toggles one of the air valves.
--[p211]-- (You may have to click twice to get a response.)

===== [p325-325] 8.14 REJECT DIE VACUUM ALIGNMENT (2块) =====
--[p325]-- 8.14 REJECT DIE VACUUM ALIGNMENT
This procedure is used to align the reject die vacuum to the inspection point to enable the removal of any rejected die.
1. Before this procedure, load the pocketed tape and align it to the inverter assembly.
2. Set the removal position in the Y direction. The Y position is set by a hard stop for the cylinder located at the end of the slide.
3. Check the position by moving the reject die vacuum forward by hand.
--[p325]-- As you do this, be careful that the vacuum device does not hit any portion of the indexer assembly. If the height needs to be adjusted, go to step 8 first, and then return to step 4.
4. Have the system move the reject vacuum assembly forward by clicking on the Actions tab. The screen shown in Fig. 8-56 will be dis-

===== [p326-328] played. Click on the Reject In/Out button located in the Others menu. (4块) =====
--[p326]-- played. Click on the Reject In/Out button located in the Others menu.
Tool Box Screen
5. Use the output optics to view through the opening in the reject vacuum assembly and check the position in reference to the pocket in the
tape. The pocket should be centered to the vacuum opening. If the position is OK, go to step 7.
6. To adjust the hard stop, press the Reject In/Out button to return the reject vacuum assembly to its resting position.
--[p327]-- Next, set the height of the reject vacuum unit. The height of the unit should be set so that it can clear the thickest die lying on the surface of the tape. This will prevent jamming when the device is attempting to remove die from the pockets.
9. To set the height of the reject vacuum unit, loosen the two locking screws on the side of the vacuum unit. (See Fig. 8-57.) Use the two socket head screws to raise the reject vacuum unit and the two setscrews to lower the unit.
--[p328]-- Lock it into place by tightening the two locking screws.
10. The reject vacuum unit is now set up. Click on the Toolbox tab, and then click on the Home All button. When the homing routine is finished, click on the OK button.
11. At this point, you should be at the Main screen. Check all pressure settings and reinstall the rubber collet tips if they are being used.
--[p327]-- Loosen the locknut, and turn the setscrew CW to decrease the stroke of the cylinder. Turn the setscrew CCW to increase the stroke of the cylinder. Tighten the lock nut. When the adjustment is complete, repeat steps 4 and 5.
7. Next, adjust the X position of the vacuum unit. This position is set by moving the adjustment knob of the slide unit mounted to the base of the machine. Adjust the position as needed. Cycle the reject vacuum unit in and out to confirm the positioning of the unit.
8.
