Table 5B-6 - Output Pocket Model Parameters   
Parameter Description   
Quality Score This item is a measure of how closely the image from the die matches the model.
--[p47]-- If the Quality Score is set too high, the system may reject all of the die. If the Quality Score is set too low, the machine may accept
For typical alignment applications, the Minimum Quality should range between “600” and “850”, depending on variations in appearance from die to die.
Brightness This changes the brightness of the image shown on the monitor for the vision system. The offset is adjustable from “5” to “255.”
--[p47]-- If the score is equal to or higher than the Quality Score figure, the vision system considers the die to be “found,” and makes the proper corrections
When you are first setting up a model, you “teach” the vision system to recognize a model, and then test the same model. Naturally, the computer returns a high quality figure. However, during the run cycle, small differences from one die to another will usually cause the quality scores to be lower.
--[p46]-- When the two images match, the vision system knows that it has found the correct location on the die. The computer judges the success or quality of the match by computing a “shape score” or minimum quality setting. The quality value ranges from “0” to “950”, where “950” indicates a perfect score or match and “0” indicates no match. When the vision system searches for an die, the die shape score must equal or exceed the Quality Score figure you
have programmed.

Waffle Columns This is the number of columns of waffle trays in the array, counted in the X direction.

===== [p182-182] 7.4 COLLET INSPECTION (1块) =====
--[p182]-- 7.4 COLLET INSPECTION
1. Open the Lexan door on the front of the unit. Check the two collets on the transfer arm. See Fig. 1-12. Look for signs of wear on the collet tips.
2. On a machine with the optional inverter, also check the inverter collets. See Fig. 1-14.
3. If any of the collets is worn, replace them all as a set. See Section 8.6 for instructions.

===== [p222-225] 8.5 INVERTER ALIGNMENT PROCEDURE OPTIONAL INVERTER (4块) =====
--[p222]-- 8.5 INVERTER ALIGNMENT PROCEDURE OPTIONAL INVERTER
1. Before beginning this procedure, complete the transfer arm setup, coincidence, collet rest height, and pick height setup. Also check the camera par focal and par centricity adjustments. Do not begin this procedure until the operations listed above have been performed.
2. From the Main screen shown in Fig. 8-11, select Maintenance/Toolbox from the top menu bar, or click on the toolbox icon at the bottom of the screen.
3.
--[p225]-- All sensors should be completely flush with the mounting block. Rotate the inverter head by hand and take note of the action of the sensors. The home sensor should remain on for the entire clockwise rotation of the head. Therefore, the flag plate must be absolutely flat and straight. It is also critithat the inverter head move freely without binding or rubbing of the flag. The importance of this cannot be stressed enough.
--[p224]-- Select Transfer Arm, then click on “0” (zero) to rotate the transfer arm to the zero position.
4. Set the inverter rotation stroke to 180,000.
5. From the Toolbox screen, activate the door dialog and open the door to deactivate drive to the motors.
6. Inspect the inverter rotating head sensors. Figure 8-12 shows the connections. Note that the front sensor is the collet 3 up sensor, the center sensor is home, and the rear sensor is collet 3 down.
--[p225]-- If the home sensor does not stay lit for the entire 180 degrees clockwise move, or if the flag binds or rubs in any way, this must be resolved before continuing.
7. Next adjust the inverter X home switch/sensor position. First verify that the home flag is centered in its travel. See Fig. 8-13.
8. From the rear of the machine, locate the inverter X lead screw. Place a .020 gauge between the lead screw nut and the motor mount block.
Turn the screw by hand to move the inverter to the end of travel with the gauge in place.

===== [p40-40] Y Inspection Error Limit (1块) =====
--[p40]-- Y Inspection Error Limit
This is the allowable error in the Y dimension when using the Output Inspection feature. After placement, the system checks the die on the waffle tray or gel pack. If a die is misplaced by more than the tolerance listed here, the machine stops and displays an error.

===== [p62-65] 1. When you select Output, and then Ink Dot Model, the system will present the screen shown in Fig. 5B-16 and Table 5B-8. (5块) =====
--[p62 [表]]-- 1. When you select Output, and then Ink Dot Model, the system will present the screen shown in Fig. 5B-16 and Table 5B-8.
Table 5B-8 - Output Ink Dot Model Parameters
Parameter | Description
Quality Score | This item is a measure of how closely the image from the die matches the model. When the two images match, the vision sys- tem knows that it has found the correct location on the die.
--[p65]-- Box Color The Black setting produces a black-on-white image. The White setting calls for a white-on-black image. If the image of the overall die produced by the camera is black with a white border, use the Black setting. If the image is white with a black border, use the White setting.
--[p64]-- When you are first setting up a model, you “teach” the vision system to recognize a model, and then test the same model. Naturally, the computer returns a high quality figure. However, during the run cycle, small differences from one die to another will usually cause the quality scores to be lower.
If the Quality Score is set too high, the system may reject all of the die. If the Quality Score is set too low, the machine may accept
--[p65]-- For typical alignment applications, the Minimum Quality should range between “600” and “850”, depending on variations in appearance from die to die.
Brightness This changes the brightness of the image shown on the monitor for the vision system. The offset is adjustable from “5” to “255.”
Contrast This adjustment changes the gain or sharpness of the image shown on the monitor for the vision system. The gain setting is adjustable from “5” to “255.”
--[p63]-- The computer judges the success or quality of the match by computing a “shape score” or minimum qual- ity setting. The quality value ranges from “0” to “950”, where “950” indicates a perfect score or match and “0” indicates no match. When the vision system searches for an die, the die
have programmed. If the score is equal to or higher than the Quality Score figure, the vision system considers the die to be “found,” and makes the proper corrections

===== [p29-29] Place Collet Puff Dwell (1块) =====
--[p29]-- Place Collet Puff Dwell
This is the length of time, in milliseconds, that the air is turned on at the place collet. This period begins after the collet is down and the vacuum is turned off. It is usually set to 10 to 25 milliseconds.

===== [p33-35] 5B.10 ABOUT OUTPUT VISION MODELS (3块) =====
--[p35]-- Edge chipping model - used to set up for edge chipping detection
• Mark model - used to set up for mark detection
The set-up process for an output model is similar to the procedure for any other model, except that Camera 2 (the output camera) is selected instead of Camera 1. Here is a quick summary:
• Set up the size and position of the model box.
• Enter the model parameters for the model.
• Train the system to recognize the model.
• Test the model.
We described the set-up process for the models in detail in Section 5.10.
--[p34]-- Pocket model - for waffle trays only: used to locate the corner of the pocket before placement
Street model - used to locate a corner of the pocket or placement location
Ink dot model - used as a backup for the Street model (notice that this is different from the usual function of the Ink Dot model)
Three other kinds of models are used for die inspection on the output side of the machine:
Bump inspection model - used to check for the correct number of bumps on the die
--[p33]-- 5B.10 ABOUT OUTPUT VISION MODELS
If the vision system will be used to inspect the output onto the waffle tray or gel pack, you must set up the output models used for this. (A "model" is an image of part of the die being placed.) On a unit with this type of output, the system can use three kinds of models to check the alignment for placement. See Fig. 5B-10.
Models Used for Output Vision - Waffle Tray Output

===== [p307-309] 8.13 SETTING INPUT LOAD/UNLOAD POSITIONS (3块) =====
--[p307]-- 8.13 SETTING INPUT LOAD/UNLOAD POSITIONS
This procedure details the setup of the load and unload positions of the input table, loader arm, and elevator axes.
1. Place a wafer carrier with a wafer frame in the first slot of the input elevator.
2. The first part of the procedure involves setting the input table to the correct position for loading and unloading frames. Select Input from the Motor Axes menu. Click on the Cycle Wafer button, and the screen shown in Fig. 8-50 will be displayed.
--[p308]-- Elevator/Loader Screen
3. Click on the XY Table to Load button. The input table will move to the load position and the stretcher assembly will open. In the ideal Y position, the input table is within .200”-.250” of the front of the frame. There should also be a .100”-.150” clearance between the front of the loader arm and the back of the stretcher plate. If the Y position is OK, skip to step 7.
4.
--[p309]-- If the Y position needs to be adjusted, it will be necessary to close the stretcher before you can jog the input table. Click on the Actions tab, and the screen shown in Fig. 8-51 will be displayed. Click on the Input box located in the Station menu. Click the Stretcher button. This will close the stretcher assembly. Click on the Tool Box tab when the stretcher has closed.

===== [p272-272] 8.8 REMOVING AND INSTALLING THEANVIL AND NEEDLE(S) (2块) =====
--[p272]-- If the tips are damaged, broken or bent, you will have to change the needle assembly. It may also be necessary to do this if you are setting up for a new product. As part of the installation process, you should check the height of the needle(s) in relation to the top of the anvil.
--[p272]-- 8.8 REMOVING AND INSTALLING THEANVIL AND NEEDLE(S)
The needles are used to release the die from the wafer film and lift them so that they can be picked by the transfer arm. The number of needles used is related to the size and weight of the die. For a small die, a single needle provides enough “lift” to raise the die clear of the film. A larger die might require a set of four or more needles.
All of the needles must be in good condition whenever the machine is picking die.

===== [p40-40] Theta Inspection Error Limit (1块) =====
--[p40]-- Theta Inspection Error Limit
This is the allowable error in the Theta direction (rotation) when using the Output Inspection feature. After placement, the system checks the die on the waffle tray or gel pack. If a die is misplaced by more than the tolerance listed here, the machine stops and displays an error.

===== [p196-196] Tool Box Function (1块) =====
--[p196]-- Tool Box Function
The Tool Box function allows the programmer or maintenance technician to move or drive many of the mechanisms used in the system. When you click on the Tool Box tab, a screen similar to the one shown in Fig. 8-1 is displayed. Two buttons on the upper right side of the screen allow the operator to select the input or output section of the machine. A list on the left side of the window shows the individual mechanisms. In the center of the window is a set of jog keys.
By selecting or highlighting a station, and then clicking on the jog keys, the operator can jog or drive that station.

===== [p276-276] Removing and Replacing the Needle Assembly (1块) =====
--[p276]-- Removing and Replacing the Needle Assembly
1. The needle assembly is held in place by an Oring. To remove the needles, no tool is required. Hold the base of the needle shaft by the cam follower and carefully pull straight up on the needle assembly.
2. Carefully insert the new needle assembly. The needle assembly will self-align using the pins located in the top of the needle shaft. Apply downward force until the bottom side of the needle mount is flush with the top side of the needle shaft.

===== [p39-39] X Inspection Error Limit (1块) =====
--[p39]-- X Inspection Error Limit
This is the allowable error in the X dimension when using the Output Inspection feature. After placement, the system checks the die on the waffle tray or gel pack. If a die is misplaced by more than the tolerance listed here, the machine stops and displays an error.

===== [p284-284] 8.10 ANVIL HEIGHT ADJUSTMENT (1块) =====
--[p284]-- 8.10 ANVIL HEIGHT ADJUSTMENT
This procedure is used to ensure that the anvil is being raised to the correct height for picking. Incorrect adjustment of the anvil may cause a misalignment of the collets during the pick and place cycle.
1. Select Anvil from the Motor Axes menu, and the screen shown in Fig. 8-41 will be displayed. Click on the Home button to ensure the anvil is starting at the correct reference point.

===== [p72-72] Inverter Puff Delay (1块) =====
--[p72]-- Inverter Puff Delay
This sets the time delay between the moment the inverter is down and the moment the puff starts, measured in milli-seconds.

===== [p30-30] SPC Inspection Interval (1块) =====
--[p30]-- SPC Inspection Interval
The programmer can set up an inspection process to ensure that the die are being placed correctly. The entry here determines how often the machine will stop and ask the operator to check the die at the output. At the start of the job, it is typical to check every 1,000 or 5,000 die. ("SPC" refers to "statistical process control".) This entry is used with SPC Pass Count listed below.

===== [p191-191] 7.9 VACUUM GENERATORS (1块) =====
--[p191]-- 7.9 VACUUM GENERATORS
1. The DS-9000 unit has several vacuum generators. Each of these generators has an ovalshaped foam filter which protects the generator’s venturi from dust and debris. If the filters are clogged, vacuum produced will be reduced from the normal 26 to 27 in Hg, and the performance of the machine may be degraded.
2. Each filter may be removed by removing the large plastic thumbscrew on the side of the housing.
3. Remove each filter and clean it with compressed air.
If the filters appear quite dirty, increase the cleaning frequency.

===== [p30-30] Place Collet On Dwell (1块) =====
--[p30]-- Place Collet On Dwell
This is a time delay, in milli-seconds, from the time the collet is down until the place puff begins.
Repeat Puffs The place puff can be set to occur in several short puffs, rather than a single long puff. This may be helpful when the die are sticky. A setting of zero turns the feature off.
Multi-puff Interval If the Repeat Puffs feature is turned on, the interval between puffs, in milli-seconds, is set here.

===== [p351-351] Table 8-3 - Controls on Pressure Switch (1块) =====
--[p351]-- Table 8-3 - Controls on Pressure Switch
Key Function
Up Increases the setting of a numerical value
R (Reset) Clears the display or clears an error
A (Auto) Auto Preset mode - not used
S (Set) Changes the input mode
Down Decreases the setting of a numerical value. This also changes the measurement units or the output mode.

===== [p41-41] Enabled (1块) =====
--[p41]-- Enabled
For this type of output inspection, the ink dot feature is used as a backup for the Street model.
(When using waffle tray output only, if the machine cannot locate the corner of the pocket using the Street model, it tries again using the Ink Dot model.) If you will be using ink dot recognition, it must be turned on here. See Section 5B.14.

===== [p183-183] 7.5 OPTICS FOR VISION SYSTEM (1块) =====
--[p183]-- 7.5 OPTICS FOR VISION SYSTEM
1. If the bulb used in one of the illuminators burn out, this will cause the pattern recognition system to fail. Slow degradation of light output can cause poor performance of the pattern recognition system, at least for some vision models. The best way to avoid this is to replace the bulbs periodically. The bulbs may usually be replaced every three months.
However, if the illuminator(s) are regularly used at or near 100% light output, it is better to replace them every month.

===== [p196-196] 8.2 ABOUT THE MAINTENANCE FUNCTIONS (1块) =====
--[p196]-- 8.2 ABOUT THE MAINTENANCE FUNCTIONS
From the Main Laurier screen, you can click on Maintenance. The system will take you to a series of maintenance functions:
Tool Box
• Motor Parameters
Actions
• Miscellaneous
We will begin this section by describing each of these maintenance functions in more detail.
